## Sessions by module

### M0 Foundations

#### 2026-10-26 · M0 Step 3: API contract split by module, generation and typed client

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: feature/api-contract-pipeline. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After pull requests #1 and #2 were merged, Sofia said "lets start step 3". Step 3 of the M0 prompt asks for:
* `contracts/openapi.yaml` with the shared parts, "referencing one file per module in `contracts/modules/`";
* `make generate` (Redocly bundle and lint, oapi-codegen per module, openapi-typescript);
* `make mock-api` with Prism on port 4010;
* the mobile API client with auth headers, one retry after a token refresh, and a typed `ApiError`.

##### What was done

* `contracts/openapi.yaml` keeps only shared parts: info, servers, default `bearerAuth` security, the `Problem` and `ModuleHealth` schemas, the `Problem` response and common headers.
* `contracts/modules/<module>.yaml` for the 8 backend modules, each starting with one tagged example path, `GET /<module>/health`, public, with an example.
* `scripts/contract-root.mjs` writes `contracts/dist/openapi.root.yaml`: the shared root plus a `$ref` to every module path. It fails on duplicate paths, operationIds or tags, on operations not tagged with their module, and on module schemas without the module prefix. It has 7 `node:test` tests.
* `redocly.yaml`: Redocly's recommended rules plus the rules generation needs, and `rule/no-sensitive-query-params`. That rule fails the build when a query parameter looks like a token, email, cédula, account, card, amount or code.
* `make generate`: contract root, `redocly lint`, `redocly bundle` to `contracts/dist/openapi.json`, then:
  * one oapi-codegen strict chi server per module (`internal/<module>/http/api.gen.go`, filtered by tag, config next to it);
  * `apps/mobile/src/shared/api/schema.gen.ts`.

  `make lint` and `make test` generate first. Everything generated is gitignored.
* `make mock-api` serves the bundle with Prism on 4010.
* Backend: each stub module mounts its generated server, so the health path answers 200 from the contract, and keeps the 501 routes. New `problem.RequestError` and `problem.ResponseErrorHandler` replace the generated defaults, which send `err.Error()` to the client.
* Mobile `@shared/api`:
  * `createApiClient` adds `Authorization`, `X-Device-Id` and a fresh `X-Request-Id`. On 401 it refreshes once (concurrent 401s share one refresh) and retries once with the body intact.
  * Failed connections become `network_error`.
  * `unwrap()` returns the data or throws an `ApiError` with the problem `code` and the request id.
  * `configureApiHooks()` is where M1 plugs in its token, refresh and device hooks.
  * The base URL comes from `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`.
* `contracts/README.md` explains to module owners how to add endpoints.
* Versions recorded in `docs/adr/0001-stack.md`.

##### Result

* `make lint test` passes from a clean clone: contract lint 0 errors (8 warnings for the missing 4xx on the example paths), ESLint, `tsc`, Prettier, golangci-lint 0 issues, Jest 105 of 105, Go 10 packages ok, script tests 7 of 7.
* Prism answers `GET /bank/health` with the contract example, and unknown paths with a problem-style 404.
* A test module with its own schema (`ConsentsConsent`) bundled with its name unchanged. The test module was removed afterwards.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.

##### Decisions and problems

* Redocly `join` was tried first. It drops the root `servers` and `security` and adds `servers: []` to every path. So a small script generates the root instead, and Redocly bundles it, as the decided stack says. The root never lists paths, so modules never edit a shared file to add an endpoint.
* Module schemas must start with the module name in PascalCase. Otherwise the bundler could quietly rename a schema when two modules use the same name.
* The generated Go package is named `httpapi` (folder `http`), so it is not confused with `net/http`.
* `@redocly/cli` 2.71.1 came out the same day and pnpm's release-age policy blocked it, so 2.71.0 is used. `@scarf/scarf` (install-time telemetry) is denied in `allowBuilds`. openapi-typescript declares TypeScript 5 as its peer but works with TypeScript 6, so it is allowed in `peerDependencyRules`.
* On Windows, `go test` sometimes exits 1 after every test passed, because another process (probably antivirus) locks the test binary (`unlinkat`). `make test-go` retries once on Windows only. Passing packages are cached and a real failure fails again, so nothing is hidden.
* The PR is about 1.650 lines, over the ~400 guideline. Roughly 700 of them are the same scaffolding repeated for the 8 modules. Splitting off the mobile client would make it depend on this PR.
* Sofia asked what her organization blocks to Claude. Nothing in this session was blocked. The two related messages were Copilot's quota notice on PRs #1 and #2 (GitHub billing) and the Supabase connector failing to connect at the start of the session.

##### Next step

Review and merge this pull request, and activate the "Main Ruleset" (it is still disabled). Then Step 4, `feature/app-shell-and-design-system`.

#### 2026-10-26 · M0 Step 4, part 3 of 3: four-tab navigation, module registry and deep links

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: feature/app-shell-navigation. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session and instruction as parts 1 and 2. This part covers what the M0 prompt asks for:
* "`RootNavigator` with an auth stack, an onboarding stack and the main tabs";
* "`src/app/registry.ts` declares every module's screens once ... with all 14 modules already listed, each exporting a placeholder screen";
* "Deep link scheme `fynk://`, with paths reserved per module ... listed in `src/app/linking.ts`".

##### What was done

* **`src/shared/navigation`.** The `ModuleRoutes` contract has four fields:
  * `tab`, the root screen of a main tab;
  * `screens`, named `<module>.<Screen>` with a path under the module's prefix;
  * `flow`, a full-screen flow such as sign in, unlock or onboarding;
  * `useGate`, which says when that flow shows.

  It also has `createPlaceholderScreen`, built from design system components.
* **Each of the 14 `src/modules/<module>/index.ts`.** It exports placeholder `routes`, its tab for the four tab owners, a flow for identity, vault and onboarding, and one placeholder screen with a deep link for the rest.
* **`src/app/registry.ts`.** All 14 modules, listed once, and the tab owners:
  * Dashboard: M6;
  * Billetera: M9;
  * Recomendaciones: M11;
  * Inversión: M15.

  It also holds the gate order (identity, vault, onboarding) and `collectScreens`, which rejects badly named or duplicate screens.
* **`src/app/linking.ts`.** The `fynk://` scheme, one reserved prefix per module, tab paths, and a check that no module declares a path outside its prefix. It keeps the prefixes other modules already use: `billetera` (M9), `metas` (M8) and `bank` (M5, for `fynk://bank/callback`).
* **Navigation.**
  * `RootNavigator`: the first open gate's flow, otherwise the tabs plus every module screen. `fynk://dev/catalogo` opens the design system catalog in development.
  * `MainTabs`, and `TabBar` like the design's bottom bar: light blue, white filled icon and dot on the active tab, 72 % white inactive tabs, and a white notch under the active tab.
* **`AppRoot`.** Loads the fonts, then the safe area, TanStack Query (no background refetch, CLAUDE.md rule 4) and the navigation container.
* **`apps/mobile/e2e/tabs.yaml`.** The first Maestro flow (opens, sees the 4 tabs, switches tabs). `make e2e` runs it.

##### Result

* Jest: 256 tests and 50 snapshots pass. The whole app renders on the Dashboard tab with the 4 tabs, and pressing Billetera switches to M9's placeholder. `make lint test` passes.
* Deep links resolve: `fynk://billetera` opens the Billetera tab and `fynk://notificaciones` opens the inbox placeholder.
* Not run: the Maestro flow and the app on a phone. There is no Android SDK or emulator on this machine, and iOS needs a Mac. The Android bundle and `expo prebuild` work.
* Pull request: opened right after this log, based on `feature/ui-components`.

##### Decisions and problems

* Gates run in a fixed order on every render, so React's hook order never changes. M1, M2 and M3 open them later from their own `index.ts`, without editing `src/app`.
* This pull request touches the `index.ts` of all 14 modules, so each module owner should look at their file.
* The routes contract (`ModuleRoutes`) is new shared API. It is not in `00-shared-contracts.md`, but the M0 prompt requires the registry. It should be added to the shared contracts doc.

##### Next step

Merge parts 1 to 3. Someone with an Android emulator or a phone runs `pnpm --filter @fynk/mobile android` and `make e2e` to check Gate A on a device. Then Step 5, `feature/local-dev-stack`.

#### 2026-10-26 · M0 Step 6: pull request CI with path filters, session log check and coverage

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: chore/ci-pipeline. PR: #9. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia asked to "proceed with step 6". The M0 prompt says:
* "`.github/workflows/ci.yml` on pull requests to `develop` and `main`: install, `make generate`, lint (ESLint, golangci-lint, redocly), unit tests (Jest, `go test ./...`), build of both Go services, `expo prebuild` sanity check, upload coverage."
* "A CI step that fails a pull request when it changes files outside `docs/` and adds no new file under `docs/history/sessions/`, with the message 'Add the session log (see CLAUDE.md, Project history log)'."
* "Path filters so a mobile only change does not run Go jobs and the other way around."

##### What was done

* **`scripts/ci-changes.mjs`.** It reads the PR diff and decides two things: whether the mobile and Go jobs run, and whether a new session log is missing.
  * It is plain Node, with `node:test` tests in `scripts/ci-changes.test.mjs` (14 tests).
  * It replaces a third-party filter action.
* **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`.** Jobs:
  * changed areas, and the session log check;
  * format and scripts (Prettier, script tests);
  * mobile: generate, ESLint, tsc, Jest with coverage;
  * Expo prebuild, for Android and iOS;
  * Go: generate, vet, golangci-lint, `go test -race` with coverage, the migration integration test on a Postgres service, `make build-go`, and `docker build` of both images.
  * "CI passed" waits for every job, so it is the one required check. Skipped jobs count as passed.
* **`.github/actions/setup-pnpm`:** Node 22.20.0, pnpm through corepack, a cached store and a frozen install.
* **Action pins.** Only GitHub's own actions, each pinned by commit SHA; each version has been out at least 7 days.
* **Makefile:**
  * `format-check` (moved out of `lint-mobile`; `make lint` still runs it);
  * `build-go` (into `bin/`);
  * `test-integration`;
  * `GOTESTFLAGS` and `JESTFLAGS`.
* **Docs:**
  * README CI section;
  * a CI table in the stack ADR;
  * `make test-integration` and an antivirus troubleshooting row in `docs/local-dev.md`.

##### Result

* **Locally:**
  * `make lint`, the Go tests, the script tests (21) and Jest (256 tests, 50 snapshots) pass;
  * `make build-go` and `make test-integration` pass;
  * actionlint finds no issues.
* **On GitHub, first push without this log.** "Session log" failed with "Add the session log (see CLAUDE.md, Project history log)", so "CI passed" failed. Every other job passed on the first try:
  * mobile, 73 s;
  * prebuild, 31 s;
  * Go, 5 min 12 s without cache (race tests, integration test and both Docker images included).
* **Coverage.** The `coverage-mobile` and `coverage-go` artifacts were uploaded. Jest covers about 97 % of lines.
* Pull request #9, opened as a draft until CI is green with this log.
* **Second push, with this log.** All 7 checks passed, including "CI passed". With caches warm the Go job took 2 min 9 s, and the whole run about 2.5 minutes.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Coverage stays on GitHub** (Sofia's choice): artifacts plus totals in the run summary. No outside service and no secret.
* **Path filters per job, not per workflow.** Otherwise a skipped workflow would leave the required check pending forever.
* **The session log check is its own job.** A missing log does not stop the code jobs, so their feedback still arrives.
* **`go build ./...` left a binary in the source folder** for a module with only one main package. `build-go` now writes to the gitignored `bin/`.
* **Antivirus holds Go tools on this laptop.** Avast behavior checks hold freshly built, unsigned Go tools, so `go test -coverprofile` (through `covdata`) and `go run actionlint` hung.
  * actionlint ran from its Docker image instead.
  * Coverage is measured in CI.
  * A troubleshooting row explains it.

##### Next step

* Merge PR #9.
* Then Sofia adds **CI passed** as a required status check on `develop` and `main`.
* The path filters get a real check on the next docs-only or mobile-only PR.
* M0 is then complete for Gate A.

  * a module health route answers 200;
  * a stub route answers 501 with `dev-token` and 401 without it;
  * both seeded users log in and get PS256 tokens with the `localhost:8081` issuer;
  * the call through Caddy to the bank succeeds with mTLS, while a direct call without a certificate is refused and the bank logs the certificate serial;

##### Decisions and problems

* Images are pinned to versions that have been out for a few days. Redis 7.4.12 and Mailpit 1.32.0 were too new.

* `make migrate` builds the API image first, so it works on its own. `migrate status` also creates empty schemas, because golang-migrate creates its table when it connects.

* `internal/app`: `All()` with the 8 modules in the contract's order, and `NewRouter` (request id, access log without query strings, recoverer, problem JSON 404 and 405, `/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/v1`).

  * `/healthz` 200;
  * `/v1/consents/x` 501 with `dev-token` and 401 without it;

* Pull request: opened right after this log.

##### Decisions and problems

* Import cycle: `internal/app` imports every module, so `Module` and `Deps` live in `internal/platform/module` and `internal/app` re-exports them.
* The stub routes require a token, so a module that lands later keeps the same auth behavior.
* `/readyz` reports only the names of the failing dependencies, because driver errors can include hosts and user names.

#### 2026-10-27 · Default transitions, back gestures and swipeable tabs

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: feature/navigation-transitions-and-gestures. PR: #29. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as #11 to #15. Sofia asked: "All transitions between screens must be smooth animations and allow gesture navigation. Manage a default and allow specifications when the user indicates them", and chose tabs that also switch by swipe. This branch is M0 Step 7.3. It is stacked on `feature/theme-components`.

##### What was done

* `src/shared/navigation/types.ts`: `ModuleScreen.presentation` replaced by `transition` (`push`, `modal`, `sheet`, `fade`, `none`) and `gesture` (`default`, `vertical`, `off`), as in shared contracts 3.6. No module used `presentation` yet.
* `src/app/navigation/screenOptions.ts`: a pure mapping to native stack options.
  * `push`: iOS-style slide on both platforms, with the full-screen swipe back.
  * `modal`: slides up and closes with a swipe down.
  * `sheet`: a native form sheet with the 32 px radius.
  * `fade` and `none`.
  * `gesture` overrides the transition's gesture.
  * Reduce Motion turns every animation into a fade and keeps the gestures.
* `RootNavigator.tsx`: every screen gets `screenOptionsFor(screen)`. Gate flows and the tabs replace each other with a fade and no gesture back.
* `app.json`: Android `predictiveBackGestureEnabled: true`, so the system back gesture shows the previous screen while swiping.
* `MainTabs.tsx`: material top tabs at the bottom with `swipeEnabled`. A tap jumps instead of sliding when Reduce Motion is on.
* `TabBar.tsx`: the notch follows the pager's `position` during a swipe.
* `@react-navigation/bottom-tabs` removed, and the ADR updated.
* Tests:
  * `screenOptions.test.ts` (8): defaults, overrides, Reduce Motion, flow fade;
  * `AppRoot.test.tsx`: tabs switch by swipe (the pager's page-selected event), and the notch sits halfway mid-swipe.

##### Result

* Tests: 19 suites, 371 tests and 100 snapshots pass. ESLint, `tsc` and Prettier are clean.
* Not checked on a device in this session: the iOS full-screen swipe, Android predictive back, and nested carousels handing the swipe to the tabs. These need a dev build on a phone.
* Pull request: #29.

##### Decisions and problems

* `ios_from_right` gives Android the same slide as iOS, so the motion looks the same on both phones.
* A tab swipe on the pager is native (`react-native-pager-view`), so it stays smooth and nested horizontal scroll views win until their edge. This needs a check on a device with the Dashboard carousel once M6 and M8 build it.
* Testing Library 14 no longer has `UNSAFE_getByType`, so the swipe test finds the native pager host (`RNCViewPager`) with `screen.root.queryAll`.

##### Next step

`feature/gesture-components` (M0 Step 7.4): `BottomSheet`, `Fab`, `Toast`, `Switch`, `SegmentedControl`, `Carousel` with `PageDots`, `PullTab`, `Accordion`, `CollapsingHeader` and `SearchField`. Then `feature/appearance-setting`.

#### 2026-10-27 · Verify Keycloak access tokens in the API (PS256, cached JWKS)

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/api-jwt-middleware. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Branch 3 of `docs/modules/M01-identity.md`: "JWKS middleware, tests with a locally signed PS256 token, remove the stub". The developer decided to "keep it as a local fallback": with `APP_ENV=local` the API still accepts M0's `dev-token`. Changes to M0 files go in separate chore PRs.

##### What was done

* New package `services/api/internal/identity/token/`:
  * `Verifier` implements M0's `auth.Verifier`. It accepts PS256 only and checks `iss`, `aud=fynk-api`, a required `exp`, and `nbf` and `iat` with 30 s of skew. `typ` must be `Bearer`, which rejects ID and refresh tokens.
  * `jwks.go` keeps the realm's PS256 signing keys for 10 minutes. An unknown `kid` refetches at most every 30 s. Without fresh keys every token is rejected.
  * `DeviceChecker` port: `X-Device-Id` is only copied to `User.DeviceID` after the identity module checks it. Until the devices endpoints exist it stays empty.
  * `WithLocalDevToken` accepts `dev-token` only when `APP_ENV=local`.
* Tests (16 plus subtests):
  * RS256, PS384, HS256 (also keyed with the public key), `none`, no `kid`, and a foreign key behind a known `kid` are all rejected
  * issuer and audience checks; an audience list containing `fynk-api` passes
  * skew at 29 s passes and at 31 s fails
  * the JWKS is cached 10 minutes; rotation; the unknown-kid throttle; fail closed
  * non-PS256 keys are ignored; device trust; end to end through `auth.Require` (401 problem JSON)
  * `dev-token` works in local only; no token or email in the logs

##### Result

* All tests pass. `go vet` and golangci-lint: 0 issues.
* One-off check: a token signed by a real Keycloak 26.8.1 with the realm of `feature/keycloak-realm-as-code` verified through this package's JWKS parsing (Java's PS256 salt length works with Go). The temporary test file and the container were deleted afterwards.
* Not installed yet. `cmd/api/main.go` and `platform/config` belong to M0, so `chore/install-keycloak-verifier` wires it in. M0's `DevVerifier` stays as the local fallback.

##### Decisions

* golang-jwt for parsing, so the JWT code is not hand-written. The JWKS cache is our own small code, because the rules are specific: 10-minute TTL, unknown-kid throttle, fail closed, PS256 keys only.
* Keys are fetched on the first request, not at startup, so the API starts while Keycloak is still starting.
* No stale keys after 10 minutes, even if Keycloak is down. This follows the prompt's 10-minute cache strictly.
* `go.mod` is left as the chore PR set it. `go mod tidy` would drop testcontainers until the realm branch merges.

##### Problems

* The test helper type `jwk` clashed with the package's own `jwk`. It was renamed `publishedKey`.

##### Next step

* `chore/install-keycloak-verifier` (M0): add `KEYCLOAK_URL` to the config, and in `cmd/api` install `token.WithLocalDevToken(token.New(...), cfg.AppEnv)`.
* Branch 4 implements `DeviceChecker` on `identity.devices`.

#### 2026-10-27 · Fix the iOS prebuild with react-native-app-auth on Expo SDK 58

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: chore/add-mobile-auth-libraries. PR: #17. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After the developer asked to open the PRs, CI's "Expo prebuild" job failed on #17 (and on #20, stacked on it): `react-native-app-auth could not find application(_:continue:restorationHandler:) in the Expo AppDelegate`.

##### What was done

* Found the cause:
  * Expo SDK 58's AppDelegate template (`expo-template-bare-minimum` 58.0.29) uses the scene based life cycle and no longer has that method.
  * `react-native-app-auth` 9.0.0's config plugin requires the method and throws.
  * 9.0.0 is the latest release.
* `apps/mobile/plugins/withAppAuthSceneLifecycle.js`: a small config plugin that adds a pass-through override of the method (it calls `super`), so the library's patch can run. It is listed after `react-native-app-auth` in `app.json`, because Expo runs mods in reverse order. Checked by running Expo's mod compiler on the SDK 58 template in both orders: only this order works.
* `apps/mobile/plugins/withAppAuthSceneLifecycle.test.js`, with the SDK 58 `AppDelegate.swift` as a fixture:
  * the template alone fails the library's patch;
  * with the plugin, the patch succeeds;
  * the method is added only once;
  * a changed template fails loudly.

##### Result

* 4 new tests pass; ESLint and Prettier are clean. The Android prebuild runs locally. The iOS prebuild can only run on macOS or Linux, so CI is the check.
* Not compiled with Xcode: there is no Mac in the team setup yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* The sign-in does not depend on this method: on iOS 13+ AppAuth gets the redirect from `ASWebAuthenticationSession` directly. The library only requires the AppDelegate to conform to its protocol, which its own patch adds.
* The plugin should be removed once `react-native-app-auth` supports the SDK 58 template. Worth an upstream issue.

##### Next step

* Rebase the stacked branches (#20, #25, #26, #27) on this fix.

#### 2026-10-27 · The rules for a user's linked devices (no database or HTTP yet)

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/devices-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer approved the plan to finish M1 and said "continue". Claude Code plans and reviews, and a Sonnet subagent writes each task ("with sonnet 5.5 do the implementation following the plan you design and verify that the work done is correct"). This part is the device rules of `docs/modules/M01-identity.md`: "The first device of a user becomes `active` at registration. Any later device stays `pending` until M2 approves it or the recovery code is used."

##### What was done

* `internal/identity/devices/device.go`:
  * the `Device`, `Status` and `Platform` types;
  * validation: a name of 1 to 64 characters with no control characters, `ios` or `android`, and a public key of exactly 32 bytes;
  * errors that never carry the name or the key, and a `String` that hides both.
* `internal/identity/devices/service.go`:
  * **Register** runs in one transaction under a per-user lock. The same Idempotency-Key or the same public key returns the existing device. A removed key is refused, so the phone makes a new key pair. The device is `active` when the user has no active device, otherwise `pending`. It audits `device.registered`, with only the platform and status.
  * **List**, **Current** (also updates the last-seen time and the Keycloak session id), **Remove**, **RemoveOthers** (for "Cerrar todas las demás sesiones"), **Usable** (the token verifier's device check) and **Activate** (for M2).
* `devices/service_test.go`: every rule on a fake repository with a fixed clock.
* `go.mod`: `google/uuid`, `golang-jwt` and `testcontainers-go` are marked as direct dependencies (`go mod tidy`). No new library.

##### Result

* `go vet`, `go test ./...` and `make lint-go` are clean.

##### Decisions

* The device name never goes into audit events or logs, because a name like "iPhone de Laura" is personal.
* After every active device is removed, a new device becomes active again. The vault key never reaches the server, so an active device without M2's approval still can't read any data.

##### Problems

* The subagent wrote its own UUID generator to avoid editing `go.mod`. Claude Code replaced it with `google/uuid`, which was already a dependency.
* A Claude Code edit made with Python wrote Windows line endings, which gofmt flagged. They were changed back to LF.

#### 2026-10-27 · The core of the 6-digit email codes, built into the Keycloak image

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-67, SCRUM-5. Branch: feature/keycloak-email-code-core. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The team decided that sign-up and password recovery use a 6-digit code sent by email, as in the design, instead of Keycloak's links. The developer asked for a secure Keycloak extension in this repository: "the extension of keycloack to follow the app flow planed in the design is a group decision that you do the respective extension it has to be still secure". Claude Code plans and reviews, and a Sonnet subagent writes each task. Limits chosen by the developer: "10 fallos en 12 h".

##### What was done

* `services/keycloak/extensions/email-code/`: a Maven project (Java 21, Keycloak 26.8.1 APIs as `provided`, JUnit 5).
* `EmailCode`:
  * issues a random 6-digit code;
  * stores only `v1.<salt>.<sha256(salt+code)>.<expires>.<attempts>`;
  * checks it in constant time, with 5 attempts, a 10-minute life and single use.
  * Input that isn't 6 digits isn't counted. A tampered note is refused. `toString` never shows the code or the hash.
* `UserLimits`, over `LimitStore`:
  * a 60 s resend cooldown and 10 wrong codes per 12 h, for each person and purpose;
  * the window starts at the first failure and does not slide.
* `SingleUseLimitStore` keeps those counters in Keycloak's single-use cache, so they hold across browsers.
* `Dockerfile`: a first stage builds the jar and runs its tests, then the jar goes into `providers/` before `kc.sh build`. The stage runs on the build machine's platform (the deploy builds arm64 with QEMU). It also works with Docker's classic builder, which testcontainers may use.

##### Result

* `mvn verify`: 19 tests pass. `docker build` works with BuildKit and with the classic builder. Claude Code ran both again itself.

##### Decisions

* The code lives only in the browser's authentication session, never on the user, so it is bound to the browser that asked for it.
* Key names are lowercased with `Locale.ROOT` (changed in review), so a server locale like Turkish cannot change them.

##### Problems

* `ARG BUILDPLATFORM` with no default breaks the classic builder, and a default of `linux/amd64` overrides the real platform under BuildKit. `${BUILDPLATFORM:-linux/amd64}` works with both.

##### Next step

* `feature/keycloak-email-verification-code`: the required action, pages, emails and realm changes on top of this core.

#### 2026-10-27 · Verify the email with a 6-digit code at sign-up and login

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-5, SCRUM-67. Branch: feature/keycloak-email-verification-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as `feature/keycloak-email-code-core`. The developer: "esta confirmacion deberia ir tanto cuando se va a crear la cuenta como si se quiere recuperar la contraseña". This branch covers account creation and an unverified login. Recovery is the next branch.

##### What was done

* **The required action `fynk-verify-email-code`** (`VerifyEmailWithCode`, `VerifyDecision`, `CodeMailer`):
  * it starts for an unverified email and sends a code;
  * it doesn't send again on a page refresh, and a "resend" honors the 60 s cooldown;
  * a wrong code shows the attempts left, the fifth asks for a new code, and after 10 wrong codes in 12 h nothing is sent or checked;
  * the right code marks the email verified and the login continues.
  * The pure decisions are in `VerifyDecision`, with 14 unit tests.
* **Events:** `SEND_VERIFY_EMAIL`, `VERIFY_EMAIL` and the errors `invalid_code`, `expired_code` and `email_send_failed`. The email and username details are removed from these events. On a send failure only one generic line is logged.
* **Pages and emails:** `fynk-email-code.ftl` (login page with form `kc-email-code-form`; HTML and text email with the code and no link) and Spanish and English messages. They work with Keycloak's default theme until the Fynk theme exists.
* **`fynk-realm.json`:**
  * `VERIFY_EMAIL` (the link) is listed but disabled;
  * the code action is enabled;
  * `registration-password-action` has `always_set_password_on_register_form: true`, so "Crear cuenta" asks for the email, the password and the terms, as in the design.
* **`realm_test.go`:** new static checks for these settings.
* **`realm_integration_test.go`:** Keycloak is now built from `services/keycloak/Dockerfile`. Tests:
  * an unverified login gets the code page, a wrong code is refused, and the right code gives `email_verified=true`;
  * registration with the password on the form, where `abcdefgh` is refused, then the code;
  * five wrong codes kill the code;
  * a resend within 60 s sends no second email;
  * the stored events carry neither the code nor the address;
  * the banco-demo realm still loads and logs in from the shared image.
* **`services/keycloak/README.md`:** the extension and the changed settings.

##### Result

* `mvn verify`: 33 tests pass. `make test-keycloak`: the whole suite passes, about 40 s with a warm image. The subagent ran it twice and Claude Code once more. `make lint-go` is clean.

##### Decisions

* The password is on the registration form, following the team's decision to match the design. Pre-hijacking someone's email stays harmless: an unverified account never gets tokens, and the owner takes it back with the reset flow (next branch), which verifies the email and ends every other session. Keycloak marks this option deprecated, and the README says to check it before upgrading.
* A failure to send the email shows Keycloak's error page instead of `context.failure()`, which Keycloak treats as a denied consent.

##### Problems

* The README still said "do not turn on `always_set_password_on_register_form` without a team decision"; it now records the decision.
* Keycloak's own `LOGIN` events still carry the username (the email). That is Keycloak's audit of logins, kept in its own database. The tests only check our events.

##### Next step

* `feature/keycloak-password-reset-code`: recovery by code, with the decoy for unknown emails, the logout of other sessions, and the design's success page.

#### 2026-10-27 · Define the fynk Keycloak realm as code, with tests on a real Keycloak

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-67, SCRUM-5, SCRUM-9. Branch: feature/keycloak-realm-as-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Branch 1 of `docs/modules/M01-identity.md`: "realm JSON, Dockerfile, local import, README with how to export changes". The developer decided against `offline_access` (open item 5), choosing "No offline_access" with 30-day sessions.

##### What was done

* `services/keycloak/realms/fynk-realm.json`: written by hand with only what differs from Keycloak's defaults. It covers:
  * a PS256 key and tokens, 5-minute access tokens, rotating refresh tokens, 30-day idle and 90-day max sessions, no `offline_access`
  * the password policy, the brute-force lock (5 failures, 15 minutes), 10-minute email links
  * events kept 90 days without the `jboss-logging` listener
  * Spanish locale
  * clients `fynk-mobile`, `fynk-api` and `fynk-admin`
  * a custom registration flow with the terms checkbox, and a user profile that cannot store a cédula
  * SMTP and secrets from environment placeholders
* `services/keycloak/Dockerfile` (Keycloak 26.8.1, built for Postgres, realms copied in) and `.dockerignore`.
* `services/keycloak/README.md`: each setting and why, the variables staging must set, reset, how to change the realm, tests.
* `services/api/internal/identity/keycloak/realm_test.go`: 17 tests on the file, no Docker.
* `realm_integration_test.go` (`-tags integration`): 11 tests against a real Keycloak 26.8.1 and Mailpit started with testcontainers. They cover:
  * password policy (`abcdefgh` rejected, `abcdefg1` accepted) and duplicate email → 409
  * PKCE required, password grant off
  * PS256 tokens of 300 s for `aud=fynk-api`, refresh rotation
  * unverified users get no code
  * sign-up with terms, verification link and first password; acceptance date and creation date recorded, no cédula
  * password reset by email with a 10-minute link
  * lock after 5 failures, events stored

##### Result

* Static tests: 17 pass. Integration tests: 11 pass, about 35 s, on Docker Desktop on Windows.
* `go vet` and golangci-lint (also with `--build-tags integration`): 0 issues.
* The image builds, and imports both the fynk realm and M16's banco-demo realm.

##### Decisions

* A short hand-written realm instead of a full export, so the pull request can be reviewed. Keycloak keeps its default flows when the file adds a custom flow (checked on a throwaway Keycloak).
* **The password is chosen after the email is verified.** Keycloak 26 does this by default while email verification is on, to prevent account pre-hijacking (someone registering your email with a password they know). It matches HU-001 ("el correo debe validarse antes de crear la cuenta"). The design's "Crear cuenta" screen has a password field, so this needs a team and design review before the theme branch.
* `requiredActions` lists all of Keycloak 26.8's defaults. A partial list replaces the defaults: the tests showed unverified users getting tokens when only `TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS` was listed. A static test now guards `VERIFY_EMAIL` and `UPDATE_PASSWORD`.
* The `jboss-logging` event listener is removed, because it writes the username (the email) to Keycloak's logs. Events stay in Keycloak's database for M13.
* `fynk-admin` gets `view-users` and `manage-users`. Keycloak cannot narrow them further, and the README says so.
* `go.mod` is left as `chore/add-identity-go-libraries` set it. `go mod tidy` would drop golang-jwt until `feature/api-jwt-middleware` merges.

##### Problems

* The terms acceptance date was not recorded at first. In 26.8.1 Keycloak only writes `terms_and_conditions` while the `TERMS_AND_CONDITIONS` required action is enabled (found by reading the Keycloak jar).

##### Next step

* `chore/keycloak-image-and-ci` (M0 files): compose builds this image and passes `FYNK_ADMIN_CLIENT_SECRET`. CI runs these tests when `services/keycloak/` changes.
* Team: confirm the "password after verification" order before `feature/keycloak-fynk-theme`.

#### 2026-10-27 · Recover the password with a 6-digit code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9. Branch: feature/keycloak-password-reset-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as the email verification branch. The developer: "esta confirmacion deberia ir tanto cuando se va a crear la cuenta como si se quiere recuperar la contraseña". For the end of the flow the developer picked "Pantalla del diseño": a success page, and the person signs in again with the new password.

##### What was done

* **The authenticator `fynk-reset-credential-code`** (`ResetCredentialWithCode`, `ResetDecision`) runs between Keycloak's `reset-credentials-choose-user` and `reset-password`:
  * it uses the same code page as the verification, the same limits (60 s resend, 5 attempts, 10 failures in 12 h) and its own budget;
  * the page has "Cambiar correo", which restarts the flow.
* **No account enumeration:**
  * an email without an account (or a disabled one) gets a decoy. It shows the same page and messages, follows the same cooldown and budget (keyed by a SHA-256 of the realm and the address) and stores a code note, but sends nothing and refuses every code;
  * the layout's "attempted username" block is hidden for the reset, because it only showed when the account existed.
* **A right code:**
  * marks the email as verified, so the owner of a mailbox takes back an account that somebody else pre-registered;
  * ends every other session of the user;
  * after the new password, ends on Keycloak's info page without logging in.
  * Keycloak's `AuthenticatorUtil.logoutOtherSessions` doesn't accept an authenticator context, so its steps are repeated with public APIs.
* **`fynk-realm.json`:** the flow `fynk reset credentials`, bound as `resetCredentialsFlow`. Keycloak's link authenticator is used nowhere.
* **Tests:**
  * `ResetDecisionTest`: the decoy subject, and a decoy that reacts exactly like a wrong code, even to the right code;
  * `realm_test.go`: the flow, its order and that the link authenticator is not used;
  * `realm_integration_test.go`:
    * the whole reset, including a wrong code, the password policy, the info page, new password in and old password out, and the old session's refresh token refused;
    * an unknown email that gets a page of the same shape and no email;
    * an unverified account that becomes verified.
  * The tests of the reset link are gone.
* **`services/keycloak/README.md`:** the recovery flow and its security rules.

##### Result

* `mvn verify` passes 39 tests. `make test-keycloak` passed twice for the subagent and once more for Claude Code. `go vet` (both tags) and `make lint-go` are clean.

##### Decisions

* The other sessions end when the code is accepted, not after the new password. Owning the mailbox is what proves who the person is.
* The decoy budget is keyed by the typed address, so trying many codes against an unknown email is limited the same way as against a real one.

##### Problems

* One difference remains: the real path sends an email and takes longer. Keycloak's own link flow has the same gap, and the README says so.
* Anyone who knows an address can spend its 10 failures and block that person's recovery for up to 12 hours. The team accepted this trade-off when it chose the limits.
* `reset-credentials-choose-user` is Keycloak's, and its own `RESET_PASSWORD` error events still carry the typed username.

##### Next step

* `chore/add-keycloakify` and the Fynk theme for the login, sign-up, code and reset pages, in light and dark.

#### 2026-10-27 · Mobile session service with one refresh at a time

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6, SCRUM-5. Branch: feature/mobile-session-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Branch 5 of the M1 plan (mobile sign-in with PKCE). The developer asked to resume after a usage limit and to "do the PRs now". The work was over the 400-line guideline, so it was split into three stacked branches. This is the first one: domain and application code, not wired into the app yet.

##### What was done

* `domain/session.ts`: `TokenSet`, `SessionStatus`, `isRefreshDue` (60 s before expiry) and `parseTokenSet` (corrupt or incomplete data gives null).
* `application/ports.ts`: `OidcPort` (authorize, refresh, endSession) with typed `OidcError` kinds `cancelled`, `rejected` and `network`, and `TokenStorePort`.
* `application/sessionService.ts`: restore from the store without opening the browser, sign in and sign up (a second tap is ignored while the browser is open), sign out (forgets locally first, then ends the Keycloak session best effort). The refresh is single flight, because Keycloak rotates refresh tokens with max reuse 0. A rejected refresh signs out, a network error keeps the session, and a refresh that finishes after a sign out is dropped.
* `application/apiHooks.ts`: the hooks M0's API client calls (`getAccessToken`, `refreshAccessToken`, `getDeviceId`).
* `application/devSession.ts`: the `dev-token` session, used only when `__DEV__` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_AUTH=dev-token`.
* Tests: session rules, 15 session service tests, and through M0's real `createApiClient` "an expired access token triggers one refresh and one retry" and "sends no token when signed out".

##### Result

* 26 identity tests pass on this branch alone; `tsc` and ESLint are clean. The whole app (309 tests) passes on the top branch of the stack.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.

##### Decisions and problems

* `signOut` now uses `try/await` around `endSession` instead of `.catch`, so a failing or misbehaving adapter can never keep the user signed in. Found because a test fake returned `undefined`.
* Split into three branches (session service, adapters, UI) to keep each PR near the size guideline.

##### Next step

* `feature/mobile-auth-adapters` (AppAuth and Keychain), then `feature/mobile-sign-in-with-pkce` (Inicio and wiring).

#### 2026-10-28 · Register the phone as a linked device and notice when it was removed

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/device-registration. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Part of the plan to "finish M1 by finishing the pending task that we left until sofia's PRs were merged". The backend devices endpoints (#71) were in `develop`, but the app never registered a device, and `deviceId()` always returned null. Claude Code (Opus) planned and reviewed the work. A Sonnet subagent wrote it, and Claude Code ran the checks again itself.

##### What was done

* **`domain/device.ts`:** the stored record and its strict parser, the display name rule (trimmed, never empty, at most 64 characters), and a base64 encoder (Hermes has no `Buffer`).
* **`application/deviceService.ts`:**
  * The first sync makes an X25519 key pair and registers it with `POST /devices`. The Idempotency-Key is saved before the call, so a retry after a crash reuses it.
  * Later syncs call `GET /devices/current`:
    * `device_removed` (410) clears the id, signs out and wipes the vault;
    * `device_not_found` (404) registers again (idempotent for the same key);
    * offline errors keep everything for next time.
  * `device_key_removed` (409) makes a new key pair and retries once.
  * Concurrent syncs share one run.
  * `forget()` drops the id on sign out, and a run that started before the sign out keeps nothing.
* **`application/deviceSessionSync.ts`:** sync on every move into signed in (sign in, sign up, restore at start); forget on sign out.
* **Adapters:**
  * the Keychain store (service `co.fynk.app.identity.device`, `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`, no cloud sync);
  * the API calls through the shared client;
  * `expo-crypto` with `@noble/curves` for the key pair;
  * `expo-device` for the model name.
* **`index.ts`:** wires it all and exports the real `deviceId()`. The dev session (`EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_AUTH=dev-token`) registers nothing.
* **`vault/index.ts` (M2, same developer):** placeholders `wipe()`, `UnlockSetting` and `RecoveryCodeRow` until M2 builds them.
* **Tests:** one test file per new file, with fakes for every port. The x25519 test checks the RFC 7748 test vector.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (512 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Not run on a phone. To check on an emulator:
  * Keychain writes;
  * X25519 on Hermes;
  * that a device removed from another phone signs this one out on its next start.

##### Decisions and problems

* **404 registers again instead of signing out.** Only 410 (removed) signs out. A 404 happens after a server reset, or when another account signs in on this phone, and signing out there would loop. Registration is idempotent for the same public key, so registering again is safe.
* **The key pair outlives a sign out, and the device id does not.** The same person signing in again gets the same device row back instead of a new pending one. Another account gets its own row with the same key, which the server allows (`unique (user_id, public_key)`).
* **Claude Code added two things in review:**
  * a guard so that a sync reading the Keychain during a sign out keeps no id, plus its test;
  * one dev-auth check in `index.ts` instead of two.
* **The private key** never leaves the Keychain adapter and the key generator. Nothing is logged.
* **The PR is about 460 lines of code plus tests,** a little over the usual size. The ports, adapters and service only make sense together.

##### Next step

`feature/mi-cuenta-screen`, after #74 (libraries) and #75 (Badge).

#### 2026-10-28 · Stamp the time of a password reset with the email code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-5. Branch: feature/email-reset-stamp. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While planning M2, Daniel chose that "No tengo ninguno de los dos" (lost phone and code) may delete the old backup only after "a password reset with the email code in the last 10 minutes". He then said "continue". The backend needs to know when that happened, and "Cambiar contraseña" must not count because it only needs the password.

##### What was done

* **Email-code extension:**
  * `ResetCredentialWithCode` stamps the user attribute `fynkEmailResetAt` (epoch milliseconds) once the reset code is verified;
  * `ResetStamp` holds the name and the format;
  * nothing else writes it: not the registration code, not `UPDATE_PASSWORD`.
* **Admin only:** the realm already has `unmanagedAttributePolicy: ADMIN_VIEW`, so the person can't see or change the attribute anywhere. The realm file is unchanged.
* **`kcadmin.Client.EmailResetAt` and `identity/ports.EmailResets`** let M2 read it. A missing user is `ErrNotFound`, and errors carry only the status code.
* **Tests:**
  * `ResetStampTest` (Java);
  * the kcadmin table tests;
  * a static realm test that the attribute stays admin-only and out of every token mapper;
  * three Keycloak testcontainers tests: a reset with the code stamps it; `kc_action=UPDATE_PASSWORD` doesn't; the person's own token can neither read it nor overwrite it through the account API.
* **`services/keycloak/README.md` and ADR 0007:** a note on the stamp.

##### Result

* `mvn verify` passes (inside `docker build --target extension`), as do `make lint-go`, `make test-go` and `make test-keycloak`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Stamped when the code is verified, not when the new password is saved.** The password step is Keycloak's own `UPDATE_PASSWORD` action. Telling a reset apart from the app's change there would need a second extension. The code already proves the mailbox, and the reset finishes in the same browser session.

##### Next step

M2's `POST /backup/reset` reads `EmailResetAt` and allows the reset only within 10 minutes.

#### 2026-10-28 · The Keycloak admin client for the profile and the device sessions

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/keycloak-admin-client. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This continues the approved M1 plan. The developer asked for a Sonnet subagent to implement the work and for Claude Code to plan and verify it. The profile step of the plan needs a client of the Keycloak admin API. It reads the profile, changes the names and ends the session of a removed device.

##### What was done

* **New package `internal/identity/kcadmin`.** It authenticates as the service account of `fynk-admin` with client credentials:
  * the secret goes in a form body and never in a URL;
  * the token is cached until 30 s before it expires, and concurrent callers share one token request;
  * every call has a 5 s timeout.
* **Methods:** `GetUser`, `PasswordUpdatedAt` (the `createdDate` of the password credential), `UpdateNames` and `DeleteSession`.
  * `UpdateNames` reads the whole user and writes it back without `attributes`. On Keycloak 26.8.1 that keeps the stored attributes, such as the terms acceptance.
  * `DeleteSession` treats a session that is already gone as success.
* **Errors:**
  * an error holds only the operation and the status code, never the body Keycloak sent back;
  * without the secret, every method returns `ErrUnavailable` and nothing goes to the network.
* **Tests:**
  * unit tests on an `httptest` server cover the form body, the cache, the refresh, one token request for concurrent callers, the 404 mapping, errors without bodies, and no secret;
  * an integration test against the Keycloak image reads a user and the date of the password. It checks that renaming keeps the email, the verified flag, the terms attribute and the password date, and that `DeleteSession` makes the refresh token fail with `invalid_grant`. A wrong secret gives `ErrUnavailable`.

##### Result

* `go vet` with both tags, `go test ./...` and `make lint-go` are clean. `make test-keycloak` passed for the subagent and again for Claude Code.

##### Decisions

* The D2 work came out at about 1,400 lines, so Claude Code split it into three stacked pull requests: this client, the profile endpoints, and revoking the session of a removed device.

##### Problems

* The terms attribute can't be set through the admin API, because the realm's unmanaged attribute policy is `ADMIN_VIEW`. The integration test accepts the terms in the browser flow instead.

##### Next step

* `feature/profile-endpoints`: `GET /me` and `PATCH /me`.

A Sonnet subagent built this and the reset pages. Claude Code reviewed the work and split it: this branch has the code page, and the next has the reset steps.

##### What was done

* **`fynk-email-code.ftl` as a Keycloakify extra page**, declared in `KcContext.ts` with the extension's attributes (`purpose`, `email`, `minutes`, `cooldownSeconds`).
  * It keeps the extension's form contract: `kc-email-code-form`, `code`, the buttons `verify` and `resend`, and the link `kc-email-code-change-link`.
* **Code boxes:** six boxes in IBM Plex Mono drawn over one real input, so paste, the one-time-code autofill, backspace and screen readers work.
  * "Reenviar en m:ss" counts down from the server's `cooldownSeconds`. The server still enforces the cooldown.
* **Verify mode:** the `OnbCorreo` content (email card, lime info box) inside the Fynk layout. This is a new composition that needs design review.
* **Reset mode:** the `Recuperar` header with its step dots, the code, and "Cambiar correo".
* **Shared flow pieces:** a compact header with a back button, the step dots, the info box, new icons, and the styles in `fynk-flow.css`. The colors come from `OnbCorreo.html` and `Recuperar.html`, in light and dark.
* **No account enumeration:**
  * Keycloakify embeds the page's whole data model in the HTML, which on this page includes `auth` and `user`, and those differ between a real account and the decoy.
  * `postBuild.ts`, Keycloakify's build hook, removes both from this page's embedded data. The build fails if Keycloakify changes the generated file so the rule can't be placed.
  * The page reads nothing from `auth` or `user`.

##### Result

* `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm format-check` and `pnpm build-keycloak-theme` pass. The jar contains `fynk-email-code.ftl` with the rule.
* Claude Code checked screenshots in light and dark, in both modes and with errors.

##### Decisions

* **Hide `auth` and `user` from this page in the build.** Keycloakify serializes the whole data model, and a React page can't repeat the extension's `<#global auth = {}>`. The build hook is the supported place to fix that.

##### Problems

* The rule has not run inside a real Keycloak yet. The branch that puts the theme in the image will run `TestIntegrationUnknownEmailLooksTheSameInTheReset` on it. That test has to ignore the per-session values in the embedded data (session codes, tab ids).
* **Needs JavaScript.** Like every Keycloakify page, this one is drawn by JavaScript.

##### Next step

* `feature/keycloak-fynk-theme-reset-pages`: the reset steps 1 and 3, the success page, and the generic info and error pages.

#### 2026-10-28 · Bring the Keycloak stack into develop and close a cooldown leak in the recovery

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-5, SCRUM-9. Branch: feature/keycloak-email-codes-and-theme. PR: #70. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer asked to "stack my PRs so is easier to merge with develop", then merged the Keycloak stack (#49 to #69) and asked "was it correctly merged?". Later: "the PR 70# doesnt pass CI".

##### What was done

* **Stacking.** Claude Code put every open M1 PR on the latest `develop` in two lines with one merge order each, wrote that order at the top of every PR, and opened #69 (the theme in the Keycloak image) on top of the Keycloak line. It rebased everything twice, because `develop` moved twice while it worked.
* **The merge left most of the stack outside `develop`.** The stack was merged from the bottom up, so only #49 reached `develop`. #50 to #69 were squashed into the branch of the PR below them.
  * This branch puts the same 18 commits on top of `develop`.
  * The tree is byte for byte the tree of #69's branch, and #49's squash in `develop` matches #49's branch exactly.
* **Cooldown leak, found by CI on #70** (`TestIntegrationUnknownEmailLooksTheSameInTheReset`). For an email with an account, the recovery code page could say 59 seconds before a new code, while for an unknown email it always said 60.
  * The real path sends the email before the page counts the seconds left, and the decoy sends nothing.
  * On CI's slower mail server, the email took long enough to cross a second boundary.
  * `ResetCredentialWithCode` now shows the full cooldown on the page that started it, the same for both.

##### Result

* `make test-keycloak` passes locally with the fix.
* #70 is open into `develop`, to be merged with "Rebase and merge" so each original change keeps its own commit.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Why a new PR instead of reopening the old ones.** The old PRs are closed as merged, and their branches already hold the squashes. A single branch with the reviewed commits is the shortest way to `develop` with nothing changed.
* **One by one is still the right way for the backend stack**, but after each merge the next PR has to be pointed at `develop` and rebased before it is merged. Otherwise it lands in the branch below it, as happened here.
* **Still open: response time.** The real path sends an email and the decoy doesn't, so a real account's recovery page can take a little longer to answer. Keycloak's own link flow has the same property. Sending the email off the request thread would close it. Not done here.

##### Next step

* Merge #70. Then the backend stack, one PR at a time. #58 gets the small change that moves its admin test to the theme's form helpers.

#### 2026-10-28 · Build the fynk theme into the Keycloak image and turn it on for login

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-5, SCRUM-9. Branch: feature/keycloak-theme-in-image. PR: none. Status: partial.

##### Instruction

The last theme step of the approved M1 plan, T3: "Realm `loginTheme: fynk` … A Dockerfile stage … the whole integration suite passes on the themed image". Claude Code (Opus) planned and reviewed. A Sonnet subagent wrote the code, and Claude Code ran every check again itself.

##### What was done

* **`services/keycloak/Dockerfile`:** a `theme` stage. It is the Maven image `maven:3.9.11-eclipse-temurin-21` with Node copied in from `node:22.21.1-bookworm-slim`; pnpm comes from `packageManager` through corepack.
  * It runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-workspace` and `pnpm build-keycloak-theme`, then copies `fynk-keycloak-theme.jar` into `providers/` before `kc.sh build`.
  * Like the extension stage, it runs on the build machine (`BUILDPLATFORM`), so the arm64 deploy build doesn't run Node or Maven under QEMU.
* **`.dockerignore`:** lets in only the theme's sources and config, never `node_modules`, build output or generated files.
* **`fynk-realm.json`:** `"loginTheme": "fynk"`. `banco-demo` keeps Keycloak's pages, and `emailTheme` stays unset until the email theme exists. The new `realm_test.go` `TestLoginUsesTheFynkTheme` checks all three.
* **Integration tests (`realm_integration_test.go`):** the theme draws the pages in the browser, so Keycloak's HTML now holds the data model (`kcContext`) instead of the form.
  * The tests read the form URL (`url.loginAction`, `url.registrationAction`), the `pageId` and the messages from the data model.
  * The recovery enumeration test compares the data model key by key:
    * two requests for the same account are identical once the session values are taken out, which proves the normalization hides nothing else;
    * a real account and an unknown email give the same first page, the same waiting page and the same wrong-code page;
    * `auth` and `user` are not on the code page.
* **"Ir a iniciar sesión" after a reset (`theme/src/login/appReturnUrl.ts`, `Info.tsx`):** Keycloak closes the login session and gives no link, and it rejects a custom-scheme base URL on `fynk-mobile`. So the page reads the app's `redirect_uri` and `state` from the `client_data` of the session URLs and links to `co.fynk.app://oauth/callback?error=access_denied&state=...`, the answer Keycloak gives when a person cancels. Only the app's own scheme is accepted. An integration test checks that `client_data` is still on that page.
* **`Info.tsx`:** the real image sends the header key `accountUpdatedTitle` on the success page, so the success page check accepts it.
* **`README.md`:** how the image builds the theme, what the tests read, and the way back to the app.

##### Result

* `make test-keycloak` passes on the themed image (64 s). `make lint-go`, the realm unit tests, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm format-check` pass.
* The subagent also checked an arm64 `buildx` build (2 min 34 s) and a cold build (2 min 6 s).
* **Pull request: stacked.** The developer asked to stack the PRs so they're easier to merge. The theme stack (#62 to #66) now sits on the email-code stack (#49, #50, #54), and this PR sits on #66: one line, one merge order.
* **Checked with the backend line too.** With the devices and profile line (#40 to #61) merged in as well, `admin_integration_test.go` (#58) no longer compiles: it uses the old form helpers. Moving it to `formAction` with page ids (`login.ftl`, `terms.ftl`) makes the whole suite pass (107 s). Whichever of this PR and #58 merges second gets that change.

##### Decisions and problems

* **`@types/node` moved to the library PR (#62).** A clean image build failed because the theme's build script needs Node types, which locally only resolved from a parent folder. A library belongs in its `chore/add-*` pull request, so the theme stack was rebased onto it.
* **No base URL on `fynk-mobile`.** Keycloak rejects `co.fynk.app://...` as "not a valid URL" in the admin API and in the realm import, so the way back is taken from the login session instead.
* **The app must take `access_denied` as "back to Inicio".** Today the AppAuth adapter would sort it as a failure. It is fixed in its own small pull request, `fix/reset-return-is-not-an-error`.
* **Size.** About 500 changed lines, 450 of them the integration tests. They can't be split off: the tests and `loginTheme: fynk` have to change together, or the suite fails.

##### Next step

* Review: Juan Pablo for the Dockerfile, because the banco-demo realm shares the image. After each merge below it, rebase onto `develop` and rerun `make test-keycloak`.
* The email theme, later.

#### 2026-10-28 · Change password from the app, and read and close the linked devices

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9, SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/password-change-and-device-list. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is part of the plan to "finish M1 by finishing the pending task that we left until sofia's PRs were merged". Seguridad (`CfgSeguridad`) has "Cambiar contraseña" and "Dispositivos activos". This branch is the data under that screen; the screen itself is the next branch of the stack. Claude Code (Opus) planned and reviewed. A Sonnet subagent wrote the code. Claude Code added the same-account check, ran the checks again, and split the work into two PRs to keep each one reviewable.

##### What was done

* **Change password:**
  * The OIDC adapter has `changePassword()`: `authorize` with `kc_action=UPDATE_PASSWORD`, and Keycloak's `kc_action_status` read from `authorizeAdditionalParameters`.
  * `SessionService.changePassword()` returns `changed`, `cancelled` or `failed`. Keycloak always answers with a new session (the browser is ephemeral, so it asks for the login first). The app keeps the new tokens and closes the old session, so a cancelled change doesn't leave two sessions open.
  * **The same account only.** On that login page someone could sign in with another account. The session compares the `sub` of the new and old id tokens (`subjectOf` in `domain/session.ts`). If they differ or can't be read, it ends the new session, keeps the old one, and returns `failed`.
  * A sign out while the browser is open keeps the app signed out. Concurrent calls share one run.
  * The dev session answers `cancelled`.
* **Profile:** `passwordUpdatedAt` is read from `GET /me` when the API sends it.
* **Devices API:**
  * `list` (`GET /devices`);
  * `remove` (`DELETE /devices/{id}`; a 404 counts as done);
  * `signOutOthers` (`POST /devices/sign-out-others`).
* **`useLinkedDevices`:** the list sorted with this phone first, `gcTime: 0`, and the two actions.
  * If this phone was removed (410), the device service signs out and wipes.
  * If it is unknown (404), the device service registers it again.
* **Domain, pure:**
  * `relativeTime` ("hace 3 meses", "ahora" under 2 minutes);
  * `sortDevices`, `otherDevices` and `sessionsLabel` ("1 sesión", "N sesiones").

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (593 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* The Keycloak side of the change is covered by `TestIntegrationAppInitiatedPasswordChange` in #78.
* Not checked on a phone.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Why the account check matters:** without it, signing in as someone else on the change page would put that account's tokens on top of this phone's vault.
* **Tokens are only decoded to compare `sub`.** The API still checks the signature of every token.
* **Splitting the work:** the whole Seguridad change was about 1,640 lines. With GitHub stacks, splitting it into the data and the screen costs nothing.

##### Next step

`feature/linked-devices-screen`: the Seguridad screen on top of this branch.

#### 2026-10-28 · Only an active device can close other sessions

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-9. Branch: fix/pending-device-cannot-remove-devices. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While planning M2, a security gap turned up in M1. Daniel approved fixing it as part of the M2 plan, then said "continue".

##### What was done

* **The gap:** `DELETE /devices/{id}` and `POST /devices/sign-out-others` didn't check the calling device. Someone who only knows the password could log in on a new phone, which starts `pending`, and remove the real phone. That phone then wipes its vault on its next start.
* **`devices/service.go`:**
  * `Remove` takes the caller's device id. A device may always remove itself, even when pending, because that is signing out.
  * Removing another device, or signing out the others, needs an active caller. A pending, removed, unknown or missing caller gets `ErrNotActive`.
  * The check runs in the same transaction and advisory lock as the removal.
  * The caller is checked first, so a pending phone can't probe which device ids exist.
* **`devices_http.go`:** `ErrNotActive` answers 403 `device_not_active`.
  * `DELETE` takes the caller from the verified `X-Device-Id`.
  * `sign-out-others` keeps the raw header, so a removed phone still gets 410 and wipes itself.
* **`identity.yaml`:** the 403 on both operations.
* **`ports/ports.go`, for M2:** the `Devices` port.
  * `Get` returns a device's name, platform, public key and status.

* The integration tests pass on a throwaway Postgres 16.15: identity, migrate, and the Keycloak testcontainers tests.

#### 2026-10-28 · Give the vault what it needs from the session and the device key

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/identity-session-for-vault. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is part of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"). The vault needs several things from identity: a fresh login to unlock without biometrics, the time of the last login, this phone's status, and an X25519 key agreement with the device key for the device approval. It must get them without ever seeing a token or the private key.

##### What was done

* **`domain/session.ts`:** `authTimeOf(jwt)` reads the id token's `auth_time`. `subjectOf` and `authTimeOf` now share one decoder.
* **`SessionService`:**
  * `lastLoginAt()` returns the time of the last typed login. Keycloak keeps `auth_time` across refreshes.
  * `reauthenticate()` runs `authorize` with `prompt=login` and `max_age=0`, and reuses `changePassword`'s same-account guard, now one `adopt()` helper. It returns `ok` only if the new `auth_time` is no older than the moment the call started, minus 60 s for clock skew. A Keycloak session that skipped the login form can't unlock the vault.
  * A sign-out during the browser step keeps the app signed out, and concurrent calls share one run.
* **Device:**
  * `currentDevice()` returns the id and status from `GET /devices/current`, handling 410 like `sync()`.
  * `devicePublicKey()` returns this phone's public key.
  * `deviceSharedSecret(peer)` does the X25519 inside identity, so the private key never leaves it.
* **`index.ts`:** exports these, plus `onSessionChange` for code outside React.
* **Seguridad:** on a pending phone, "Cerrar" and "Cerrar todas las demás sesiones" are hidden and "Este dispositivo espera aprobación" is shown. The API now refuses those calls from a pending phone.
* **Tests:**
  * `authTimeOf`;
  * `lastLoginAt` across sign in, refresh and sign out;
  * `reauthenticate`: ok, cancelled, failed, another account, a stale or missing `auth_time`, sign-out during the browser step, concurrent calls;
  * `currentDevice`;
  * the RFC 7748 §6.1 vectors for the shared secret;
  * the exports;
  * the pending phone's Seguridad, in light and dark.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The fresh-login check was added during review.** Without it, `ok` would only mean "the browser came back with the same account".
* **The dev session** answers `ok` with `lastLoginAt` set to now. It has no device, so the device functions return null or reject.

##### Next step

The vault's lock screen and biometrics call `reauthenticate()` and `lastLoginAt()`. The device approval uses `deviceSharedSecret()`.

#### 2026-10-28 · Write the backup module's API contract

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62, SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/backup-contract. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel pasted the M2 prompt: "You are building module M2 of Fynk: the encrypted vault on the phone, biometric unlock, the zero knowledge backup of manual data, and moving that data to a new phone." He approved the plan and said "continue". This contract is the plan's first pull request, so the backend and the app can both build on it.

##### What was done

* **`contracts/modules/backup.yaml`:** the nine endpoints of the shared contracts §5, plus two new ones decided in planning. M0's health example is removed. The contract covers:
  * the encrypted records: at most 200 per call, an atomic batch, `stale_version`, a 5 MB quota, and a cursor page;
  * the recovery key: PUT only from an active device. GET never returns the proof hash;
  * `POST /backup/recovery-key/activate`: a pending phone proves it opened the wrapped key, with no decryptable answer and 5 tries per hour;
  * `POST /backup/reset`: only after a password reset by email code in the last 10 minutes;
  * the device approvals: create, the pending list for the old phone, the state for the new phone, approve and reject.

  Every operation needs `X-Device-Id`, and every response has an example for Prism.
* **`internal/backup/module.go`:** the generated server isn't mounted yet, so every route answers 501 through `module.Stub`. The implementation pull requests mount it.
* **`internal/app/app_test.go`:** skips backup in the health path test, as it already does for identity.
* **`00-shared-contracts.md`:** the two endpoints in §5, and `backup.reset` in the audit event list (§4.3).
* **`M02-vault.md`:** one line each for the two endpoints.

##### Result

* `make generate` and `make lint-go` pass (0 issues), as do `make test-go` and `make format-check`.
* Prism serves the new routes with their examples.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Activation by proof (decided with Daniel):** a phone that restores with the code becomes active only if SHA-256 of `HKDF(DEK, 'fynk/device-proof/v1')` matches the hash saved with the recovery key. Otherwise anyone with the password could activate a phone and overwrite the backup and the recovery key.
* **Reset after an email-code password reset (decided with Daniel):** "No tengo ninguno de los dos" deletes the old backup. Requiring the email code means a password alone cannot destroy someone's backup.
* **The approval state enum is its own schema** (`BackupApprovalState`). The inline enum gave oapi-codegen a duplicate type name.
* **No handler is mounted yet,** so no hand-written 501 responses are needed.

##### Next step

The backend stack implements the endpoints, and the app builds on the generated types.

#### 2026-10-28 · Store the encrypted backup records

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-records-store. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the next step of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): "PUT /backup/records … 409 stale_version when the version is lower, a 5 MB per-user quota counted with octet_length in the transaction". This PR is the storage half. The HTTP endpoints are the next PR of the stack.

##### What was done

* **Migration 2:** a `seq` column from `backup.records_seq`, indexed by user. It is the paging cursor; timestamps are not safe across API instances.
* **`records/`:**
  * the envelope, the limits and the opaque cursor (base64url of the seq, decoded strictly);
  * the service: only an active device may read or write, a batch is stored all or nothing, and `backup.uploaded` is audited with the count only.
* **`postgres/records.go`:**
  * a per-user advisory lock serializes writes and page reads, so seq order is commit order and a page never skips a row committed later;
  * the version check is in the upsert itself (`WHERE version <= EXCLUDED.version`), so a lower version stores nothing and becomes `stale_version`;
  * the quota sums ciphertext plus nonce bytes in the same transaction.
* **Tests:** the service against fakes (pending, removed and unknown devices, stale, quota, cursors, audit meta), and the migration version check.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass for the backup module, alone on this commit.
* The Postgres integration tests pass with `-p 1` on Postgres 16.15.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The implementer stopped on the weekly usage limit** and was resumed after it reset. Its work was split into three PRs to stay near 400 lines: this one is 527 lines, 206 of them tests.

The HTTP endpoints (`feature/backup-records-endpoints`).

#### 2026-10-28 · Backup schema and the recovery key rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-recovery-key-store. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the next step of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): the backend schema and the rules for the recovery key, including activating a new phone with proof of the key.

##### What was done

* **`backup/migrations`:**
  * the three tables of the M2 prompt: `records`, `recovery_keys` and `device_approvals`;
  * `recovery_keys.device_proof_hash`, the SHA-256 of `HKDF(DEK, 'fynk/device-proof/v1')`, never returned;
  * CHECK constraints on the key sizes (48, 12, 16 and 32 bytes);
  * indexes for paging records and for finding approvals.
* **`backup/recovery`:**
  * **`Set`:** only an active device; it replaces the old key, so the old code stops working, and audits `recovery_key.set`.
  * **`Get`:** never returns the hash.
  * **`Activate`:** compares `sha256(proof)` with the stored hash in constant time. On a match it activates the device through identity's `Activator` and audits `device.approved` and `backup.restored` with `method=recovery_code`.
  * **The failure limit:** 5 failed proofs per user per hour. Redis counts them when it's configured, otherwise an in-process rolling window does.
* **`backup/postgres`:** the repository, an upsert.
* **`module.go`:** the schema is migrated. Every route still answers 501 until the endpoint PR mounts them.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passes (0 issues), and so does `make test-go`.
* The Postgres integration tests pass on a throwaway Postgres 16.15. They cover replacing a key, isolation per user and the size check.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Split from the endpoints** to keep each pull request under 400 lines. The handlers and the HTTP integration test come in the next PR of the stack.
* **The activation limit is checked before the comparison and counted only on a mismatch.** Two parallel guesses could slip past the count by one. That is acceptable because the proof is 256 bits derived from a 128-bit code: the limit is a second line of defense, not the main one.
* **A failed audit write after activation answers 500.** The device stays active, and a retry is a no-op. This is the same order identity uses.

##### Next step

The endpoint PR mounts `PUT` and `GET /backup/recovery-key` and the activate route.

#### 2026-10-28 · The SQLCipher vault engine

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-8. Branch: feature/sqlcipher-vault. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is step 1 of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): "VaultDb, migrations runner, key copy A, lock states, wipe. Test that the file cannot be read without the key." The lock states come in the next PR.

##### What was done

* **`adapters/sqlite/opSqliteDriver.ts`:** op-sqlite with SQLCipher.
  * op-sqlite passes `encryptionKey` straight to `sqlite3_key_v2` (read in `OPBridge.cpp`). The DEK goes as SQLCipher's raw key `x'<64 hex>'`, so there is no passphrase derivation on top.
  * op-sqlite is loaded lazily, so importing `@modules/vault` touches no native code.
* **`application/vault.ts`, the engine:**
  * **Refuses to open when `PRAGMA cipher_version` is empty.** A build without SQLCipher must never write a plain file. It then reads `sqlite_master`, so a wrong key fails at once.
  * **Runs each module's migrations** in registration order, tracked in `vault_migrations(module, version, applied_at)`. It checks the `<module>_` table prefix and the versions. A module that registers after unlock gets its tables right away.
  * **One queue for everything:** transactions, migrations, and statements outside a transaction. A statement never lands inside another module's open transaction, where it would be rolled back with it.
  * **A kept `VaultDb` throws `VaultLockedError`** once the vault is closed.
  * **`wipe()`** closes, deletes the file and always removes both copies of the key, even if the delete fails. Without its key, a left-over file can never be read. It then emits `vault.locked`.
* **`adapters/keychain/dekStore.ts`:**
  * copy A in `co.fynk.app.vault.key`, `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`, no cloud sync;
  * `co.fynk.app.vault.key.biometric` is reserved for copy B;
  * a corrupt entry reads as no key.
* **`index.ts`:** the real `registerVaultMigrations`, `getVaultDb` and `wipe`. M1 already calls `wipe` on a removed phone; with no vault yet it just clears the keychain.
* **Tests:**
  * the engine on a real SQLite (sql.js), plus a fake for the SQLCipher check;
  * the op-sqlite open options and pragmas, with a mock;
  * the keychain options;
  * random DEK creation.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (50 suites, 682 tests) and `make format-check` pass.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Three fixes made during review:**
  * statements outside a transaction now wait in the queue;
  * a kept `VaultDb` checks the lock;
  * `wipe` removes the keys in a `finally`.
* **"The file cannot be read without the key" can only be shown on a phone.** SQLCipher is native and Jest runs on Node. On a phone: open `fynk-vault.db` with `sqlite3` and expect "file is not a database". The `cipher_version` guard stops a build without SQLCipher from writing anything.
* **Nothing opens the vault yet.** The lock screen PR wires it to the session.
