#### 2026-10-28 · The vault's key hierarchy as pure functions

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-8, SCRUM-62, SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/vault-key-crypto. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

This is the first code of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"). The prompt's key hierarchy becomes pure, tested functions: "BK backup key = HKDF-SHA256(DEK, info 'fynk/backup/v1')… Recovery code: 128 random bits shown as 26 Crockford base32 characters in 5 groups… 6 digit approval code: the first 4 bytes of SHA-256 of the new device public key…".

##### What was done

* **`domain/crypto.ts`:**
  * `seal` and `open`: AES-256-GCM through `@noble/ciphers`, with AAD. Any change to the ciphertext, tag, nonce, key or AAD throws `VaultCryptoError`, which says nothing about the data.
  * `deriveBackupKey` and `deriveDeviceProof`: HKDF-SHA256 with `fynk/backup/v1` and `fynk/device-proof/v1`.
  * `deviceProofHash`.
  * `recordAad`: `userId|collection|recordId|version`.
  * `approvalCode`: the 6-digit code from the new phone's public key.

  No randomness inside: keys and nonces are parameters.
* **`domain/recoveryCode.ts`:**
  * `encodeRecoveryCode` (16 bytes to 26 characters) and `formatRecoveryCode` (5, 5, 5, 5 and 6);
  * `parseRecoveryCode`: accepts spaces, hyphens and lowercase, maps I and L to 1 and O to 0, and rejects U, other characters, a wrong length and non-zero padding;
  * `decodeRecoveryCode`.
* **Tests, all with fixed vectors:**
  * GCM checked against Node's `createCipheriv` and NIST GCM test case 16, and tampering with every part fails;
  * HKDF checked against Node's `hkdfSync`;
  * the approval code of 32 zero bytes is `123181`;
  * the recovery code of `00 01 … 0F` is `000G40R40M30E209185GR38E1W`, checked independently with Python;
  * round trips, tolerant parsing and rejections.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass on the vault branch above this one (50 suites, 682 tests).

##### Decisions and problems

* **Split from the vault engine** to keep each pull request under 400 lines. The engine is the next PR of the stack.
* **The proof's comment says the server sees it once.** A new phone sends it to be activated, and it is one-way: the DEK can't be derived from it.

##### Next step

The SQLCipher engine (`feature/sqlcipher-vault`).

##### Decisions and problems

* **op-sqlite 18.2.5, not 19.0.2.** 19 came out two days ago with the same peers.
* **react-native-argon2 is a legacy bridge module.** React Native 0.88 loads it through the interop layer. It has to be tried on a phone, with the pure JavaScript Argon2 of @noble/hashes as the slow fallback.
* **The SQLCipher flag only shows in a real build.** The build log should print `[OP-SQLITE] using sqlcipher.`. The vault code will also refuse to open when `PRAGMA cipher_version` is empty.

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-8, SCRUM-6. Branch: feature/vault-lock-controller. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Step 2 of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("continue"): "lock states locked, unlocking, unlocked; AppState: lock after 5 minutes in the background, and on sign out … unlock with copy A when lastLoginAt() is under 5 minutes old; otherwise 'Ingresar con contraseña' calls reauthenticate(); the setup decision of decision 4".

##### What was done

* **`domain/lockPolicy.ts`:** `shouldLock` (more than 5 minutes in the background; exactly 5 doesn't lock) and `isFreshLogin` (a login of the last 5 minutes, tolerating a phone clock up to 5 minutes behind).
* **`application/lockController.ts`:**
  * the states `locked`, `unlocking`, `unlocked`, `pendingDevice` and `error`;
  * **with copy A,** it opens only after a fresh login; otherwise it stays locked until `reauthenticate()` answers `ok`;
  * **without a key,** a pending phone waits for approval, and only an **active** phone gets a new DEK and copy A;
  * it locks on sign out and after more than 5 minutes in the background. A lock during an open closes the vault again;
  * a build without SQLCipher shows `notEncrypted` and is never retried.
* **Identity (M1, SCRUM-6):** `currentDevice()` waits for this sign in's registration before reading. The dev session's phone counts as active.
* **Tests:** the policy, and every state change with fakes and a fixed clock.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (58 suites, 803 tests on the top branch) and `make format-check` pass. This commit passes alone too.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The implementer stopped on the weekly usage limit** and was resumed after it reset.
* **A bug found in review:** `currentDevice()` is `null` while the phone is still registering, when the API doesn't know it, or right after it was removed. The controller treated `null` as the first phone and made a new DEK: a second phone could get its own key before the API had marked it pending, and a removed phone got a key right after its wipe. Now only `active` makes a key, `null` is an error with a retry, and identity waits for the registration first.
* **A reinstall on an active phone with a recovery key on the server** still counts as the first phone here. The recovery code PR asks the server first. Nothing uploads to the backup before then.
* Split into three PRs (controller, screen, gate) to stay near 400 lines. This one is 632 lines, 444 of them tests.

#### 2026-10-29 · The backup reset rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-reset-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Decision 2 of the M2 plan Daniel approved ("Email code reset"): "No tengo ninguno de los dos" needs a password reset by email code in the last 10 minutes. `POST /backup/reset` then deletes the backup and the recovery key, removes every other device and revokes its sessions, activates this phone, and audits `backup.reset`.

##### What was done

* **`reset/service.go`:**
  * the caller's phone may be pending or active, but not removed or unknown;
  * the email-code reset must be at most 10 minutes old, and not more than 1 minute in the future;
  * the caller's Keycloak session must have started after the reset, with 60 seconds of skew;
  * then, in order: the backup is deleted, the other devices are removed and their sessions revoked, a pending caller is activated, and `backup.reset` is audited with `method=email_code`. Every step is idempotent, so a retry within the window finishes the job.
* **`postgres/reset.go`:** one transaction takes the records lock and the approvals lock, then deletes the user's records, recovery key and approvals.
* **`backup.reset`** is defined in the backup module. `internal/audit` belongs to M13 and wasn't touched; the constant can move there later.
* **Tests:** every refusal, including the exact window and skew edges, both callers, and a failure at each step.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass on this commit alone.

##### Next step

The endpoint (`feature/backup-reset`).

#### 2026-10-29 · The device approval rules

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/device-approval-service. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Step 3 of the backend plan: "approve calls ActivateDevice; audit device.approved and device.rejected; tests for 'expired cannot approve' and 'not active cannot approve'".

##### What was done

* **Create:** only a pending phone asks. An active phone gets `ErrAlreadyActive`, and a removed or unknown one `ErrNotActive`. The public key comes from identity, never from the request. A new request expires the phone's older open ones.
* **Pending list:** only for an active phone, newest first, skipping requests whose phone was removed or is no longer pending.
* **Get:** only the phone that asked can read its request; any other caller gets not found. A request past its 10 minutes reads as expired. **The wrapped DEK is returned once:** the same transaction clears it and audits `backup.restored` with `method=device_approval`, so a failed audit keeps the key for a retry.
* **Approve and reject:** only an active phone. Not found, not pending, or expired (410) are checked under a row lock, and the new phone must still be pending. Approving stores the grant and activates the new phone before the commit, so a failed activation leaves the request pending. `device.approved` and `device.rejected` carry the method and the deciding device, never key material.
* **Tests:** every rule with fakes and a fixed clock.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test` pass on this commit alone.

##### Decisions and problems

* **An expired request is stored as expired, and then 410 is returned.** The status is committed first, because an error inside the transaction would roll it back.
* **Known gap:** activation runs on identity's own connection. If the approval's commit fails after it, the phone is active and the request stays pending. That phone can still restore with its recovery code.
* **An approved grant never read** stays until the new phone reads it. Only that phone's private key can open it.
* This PR is 789 lines, 522 of them tests.

##### Next step

The endpoints (`feature/device-approval-endpoints`).

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/approve-new-device. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the old phone's side of HU-046 on top of `feature/backup-sync`: the approval crypto fixed by ADR 0002, the `/device-approvals` API adapter, the service that seals the DEK for the new phone, an owner check with biometrics or the password, the "Aprobar nuevo dispositivo" screen, an in-app notice, and an "Aprobar" action in M1's Seguridad. The new phone's side belongs to the restore branch.

##### What was done

* **`domain/deviceApproval.ts`:** `approvalKey` (HKDF-SHA256 of the X25519 shared secret, info `fynk/device-approval/v1`), `sealGrant` and `openGrant` with the lowercase approval id as AAD. Tests use vectors made with Node's crypto and a round trip with real X25519 on both sides.
* **`adapters/api/deviceApprovalsApi.ts`:** the five operations with base64 only in the adapter; errors become `ApprovalError(kind)` in `application/ports.ts`.
* **`application/approveDevice.ts`:** `listPending` (adds the 6 digit code), `approve` (owner check, ephemeral key, shared secret, seal, send, wipe), `reject`. X25519 sits behind a `KeyAgreementPort` (`adapters/crypto/x25519KeyAgreement.ts`).
* **`adapters/confirmOwner.ts`:** copy B behind the prompt when it exists, else a fresh Keycloak login and copy A. It always asks, even with the vault open.
* **`ui/ApproveDeviceScreen.tsx`:** polls every 5 s only while focused; card per request with device, platform, code 3+3, minutes left, Aprobar and Rechazar, and every message. Registered as `vault.ApproveDevice`, path `boveda/aprobar`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No `isEnabled` check in the notifier:** M12 keeps `device_approval` always on, so a security notice never depends on a switch.
* **A failed password check throws and the service answers `failed`;** only a cancel answers `cancelled`.
* **An invalidated copy B falls back to the password** without deleting copy B; the lock screen does that on its next use.
* **A request whose public key is not 32 bytes is hidden** by the adapter, since it could not be approved.
* **`notFound` is reported as `notPending`:** for the person, the request is gone.
* **The Seguridad route is the literal `identity.Security`** in the vault index, because identity is still loading when the vault index loads (import cycle).
* The new screen and the "Aprobar" button on the device row are not in the design: they need design review.

##### Next step

The restore branch: the new phone creates the request, shows the code, polls and opens the grant.

#### 2026-10-30 · The backup record envelope and registry

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-registry. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel asked to "continue the implementation of M2 and complete what is left". This is the first half of the backup engine on the phone: seal each record, know which collections take part and which records changed, and talk to `PUT` and `GET /backup/records`.

##### What was done

* **`domain/backupEnvelope.ts`:**
  * `backupRecordId`: a UUID stays, and any other id maps to a stable version 8 UUID from SHA-256 of `collection|id`;
  * `sealRecord` and `openRecord`: AES-256-GCM with the record AAD, the plaintext `{id, data}`, and a typed error over 64 KiB.
* **`domain/base64.ts`:** standard base64 with padding, a copy of identity's helpers.
* **`adapters/vault/migrations.ts`:** the table `vault_backup_dirty`, registered in `defaultVault.ts`.
* **`application/backupRegistry.ts`:**
  * `registerBackupCollection` and `markDirty`, with the same rules as the in-memory registry of `@shared/testing`;
  * marks made while locked wait in memory and are written on unlock;
  * an upload clears only the marks it saw.
* **`adapters/api/backupRecordsApi.ts`:** the records API, with base64 fields, `X-Device-Id` and `BackupError` kinds.
* **M1:** `userId()` exported from `@modules/identity` (the `sub` of the session), for the record AAD.
* **Tests:**
  * envelope vectors and tamper cases (ciphertext, nonce, user, collection, id, version, key);
  * a check that the ciphertext never contains the plaintext;
  * the registry rules, the dirty table, and the API with a fake fetch.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.
* Nothing uploads yet. The queue, restore and public exports come in `feature/backup-sync`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The `deleted` flag is not in the AAD,** so `openRecord` also checks that the content agrees with it. A server that flips the flag cannot delete or resurrect a record.
* **`markDirty` is synchronous** and only queues the write, so a module may call it inside its own vault transaction without waiting on it.
* The work was done by a Sonnet 5.5 agent and reviewed by the orchestrating session, which split it from the upload and restore half to keep each pull request reviewable.

##### Next step

The upload queue and restore (`feature/backup-sync`).

#### 2026-10-30 · Upload and restore the encrypted backup

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/backup-sync. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Daniel asked to "continue the implementation of M2 and complete what is left". This is the second half of the backup engine on the phone. It uploads the changed records while the app is open and restores everything on a new phone, against the finished `PUT` and `GET /backup/records`.

##### What was done

* **`application/backupSync.ts`:**
  * the upload runs 5 s after the last change, and at once when the app leaves the foreground;
  * it sends batches of 200, one upload at a time, and clears only the marks it saw;
  * a 409 downloads the newer versions, imports them and retries once;
  * `restoreAll` downloads every page and keeps the highest version of each record;
  * `exportAll` is for M13's data export.
* **`adapters/appStateBackup.ts`:** the AppState watcher for the background flush.
* **`adapters/defaultBackup.ts`:** the default registry and sync, reading copy A for the backup key.
* **`index.ts`:**
  * exports `registerBackupCollection`, `markDirty` and `exportAll`;
  * `wipe()` clears the backup state;
  * `restoreAll` stays inside the module for the restore screens.
* **Tests (fake timers and a sql.js vault):**
  * the debounce, the background flush, 201 records split 200 + 1, and a mark during an upload;
  * 409, 403 and offline;
  * restore paging (a full last page, then an empty one), the highest version, and unknown collections;
  * marks made while locked.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (116 suites, 1447 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The key cache lives in the sync,** and is dropped on `vault.locked` and on wipe, so it never outlives an unlocked vault.
* **A record the API would refuse (over 64 KiB) stays dirty** and does not block the rest of the batch.
* **The 409 retry works because the server refuses only a lower version than the stored one.** After the import, the local version is at least the remote one.
* **`importAll` is called only for collections that have records** in the download.
* **New error kind `noKey`** for `restoreAll` when the key or the account is missing.
* **A server that repeats its cursor** stops the restore with an error instead of looping.
* **The dev session has no registered device,** so the backup stays off there.
* **Only a phone can check** the dirty table upsert on SQLCipher, the AppState flush, and the real quota and cursor.
* The work was done by a Sonnet 5.5 agent and reviewed by the orchestrating session, which split the branch in two to keep each pull request reviewable.

##### Next step

The restore screens on a new phone (`feature/restore-with-recovery-code`), which call `restoreAll` after the phone gets the DEK.

#### 2026-10-30 · The recovery code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62, SCRUM-5. Branch: feature/recovery-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build HU-045 on the finished backend (`PUT` and `GET /backup/recovery-key`): make the recovery code on the first phone, wrap the DEK with a key from the code, show the code once and ask for its last group before uploading. Also "Generar un código nuevo" in Seguridad, and the M1 criterion of SCRUM-5, "Al terminar el registro se genera el código de recuperación".

##### What was done

* **Domain:** `recoveryKey.ts` (`wrapDek`, `unwrapDek` with the AAD `fynk/recovery/v1`, the contract's Argon2 parameters), `matchesRecoveryGroup` in `recoveryCode.ts`, and `base64.ts` for the API fields.
* **Ports and adapters:** `RecoveryKdf`, `RecoveryKeyApi`, `RandomBytes`, `RecoveryError`; `argon2Kdf` over `react-native-argon2` and `recoveryKeyApi` over the shared client.
* **Service:** `createRecoveryCodeService`. `prepare(dek)` makes the code, derives the key and wraps the DEK, and `commit()` is the only upload. `confirmRecoveryCode` compares the typed last group and then commits.
* **Lock controller:** new state `recoveryCode`. The first phone asks the server first: a recovery key means a backup exists, so it makes no key (`pendingDevice`), and a failed question is `offline`. After any open with a key, an active device asks the server and shows a new code when there is none. A network error leaves the vault unlocked.
* **UI:** `RecoveryCodeScreen` (two steps), shown by `VaultFlow` and by the pushed `NewRecoveryCodeScreen`; `RecoveryCodeRow` under "Desbloqueo" in Seguridad. The Maestro signup flow now saves the code.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (119 suites, 1445 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Not run: the Maestro flow, which needs an emulator and the local Keycloak.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The check after an open does not block it.** The vault shows as unlocked at once and the server check runs afterwards, so an offline phone opens normally. When the answer is "no recovery key", the flow replaces the tabs. This can flash the tabs for a moment.
* **The password step of "Generar un código nuevo" runs when the screen opens**, not on the row's tap, so the `boveda/recuperacion` deep link cannot skip it. `UnlockSetting` asks inside the controller on the action; this one asks inside `prepareNewRecoveryCode`.
* **`react-native-argon2` is loaded on first use** (`require` inside `argon2Kdf`). Its native module is read while the library loads, which broke the identity tests that import the vault index.
* The shared `fonts` token has no "mono" family; the code uses `fonts.amount.semibold` (IBM Plex Mono).
* **The dev session has no backup.** With `EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_AUTH` there is no registered device and no API, so asking the server for the recovery key failed and the vault never opened (dev builds and `make e2e`). Identity now exports `backupEnabled()` (false in the dev session) and the lock controller takes it as a dep: when it is false the first phone opens as before with no recovery code, the check after an open returns at once, and "Generar un código nuevo" fails.
* The Argon2 password is the canonical 26 character code. The parameters are the contract's and were not changed.

##### Next step

The restore on a new phone with the recovery code, and the backup records sync.

#### 2026-10-30 · Restore on a new phone with the old phone's approval

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-63. Branch: feature/restore-with-approval. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the new phone's side of HU-046: "Aprobar desde mi otro teléfono". The new phone asks, shows a 6 digit code, waits for the old phone to approve, opens the grant and restores like the code path does. Share the steps after the DEK is known between the two paths.

##### What was done

* **`completeRestore.ts`:** the part of `restoreWithCode` that starts once the DEK is known (wipe, copy A, open, mark, `restoreAll`, clear the mark, undo on failure). Both paths use it; the `restoreWithCode` tests did not change.
* **`restoreWithApproval.ts`:** `start()` makes a random Idempotency-Key, creates the request and returns the id, the code and the expiry (`device_already_active` becomes `alreadyActive`). `check(id)` is one poll: `waiting`, `rejected`, `expired`, `offline`, or, when approved with a grant, shared secret, `openGrant` with the approval id as AAD, `completeRestore`, and the secrets zeroed. Approved without a grant is `grantLost`.
* **`lockController.ts`:** `pendingDevice` carries `deviceStatus` (`pending` or `active`). `startApproval()` and `checkApproval(id)` set `restoring` while the restore runs and `unlocked` at the end. After `failed` or `grantLost` the phone is `active`.
* **UI:** the option card, `ApprovalWaiting` (instruction, code 3+3, "Esperando aprobación…", "Cancelar") and the `useApprovalRestore` hook that polls every 5 s and stops on cancel, a final answer, the expiry and unmount. Messages for rejected, expired, grantLost, offline and failed, with "Pedir de nuevo" for the first two.
* **Wiring:** `defaultLock.ts` passes the approvals API, `devicePublicKey` and `deviceSharedSecret` from identity.
* **Tests:** every outcome of the service with fakes, a full round trip with the real old phone service and an X25519 ephemeral key, the controller states, the screen in light and dark with fake timers.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (134 suites, 1769 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.
* It needs a real pair of phones to see the whole flow.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Option hidden for `active`:** the server refuses an approval for an active phone, so only the code is offered. A 409 at start makes the controller switch to `active` too.
* **`onRestoring` fires when the grant arrives**, not when the restore starts: the server clears the grant on that read, so any failure after it leaves an active phone with no key.
* **Offline during a poll** keeps waiting and shows a small notice.
* **Cancel** only leaves the screen; the request stays open on the server until it expires (10 minutes).
* **Only a phone can check:** the real X25519 key in identity, the old phone showing the same code, and the timing of the poll.

##### Next step

"No tengo ninguno de los dos" (`feature/restore-without-code`).

#### 2026-10-30 · Restore on a new phone with the recovery code

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/restore-with-recovery-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

HU-045: "Restore on a new phone with the recovery code". The phone that is pending, or active with no local key while the server has a recovery key, must let the person type the code. A wrong code is found on the phone; only a right one activates the device, writes copy A, opens the vault and downloads the backup.

##### What was done

* **API:** `RecoveryKeyApi.activate(proof)` over `POST /backup/recovery-key/activate`. The errors become `RecoveryError` kinds `mismatch`, `tooMany`, `removed` and `noKey`.
* **`restoreWithCode.ts`:** parse the code, read the recovery key, derive RK, open the DEK on the phone (wrong code ends here, the server hears nothing), send the device proof, then wipe the old file, write copy A, open the vault, set the restore mark, `restoreAll`, clear the mark. RK, proof and DEK bytes are zeroed at the end.
* **Restore mark:** vault migration 2 creates `vault_state`; `restoreState.ts` sets, reads and clears `restore_pending`. After any open with the backup on, the lock controller resumes a restore that was cut short, in the background.
* **Lock controller:** new state `restoring`, new `restoreError` on `pendingDevice`, and `restoreWithCode(typed)`. Wired in `defaultLock.ts` with `restoreAll` from `defaultBackup.ts`.
* **UI:** `RestoreScreen.tsx` replaces the static pending text. Title, text, the "Usar mi código de recuperación" option, the code field, "Restaurar", the error texts and the "Cerrar sesión" link. Two comment slots mark where the next branch adds "Aprobar desde mi otro teléfono" and "No tengo ninguno de los dos".

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` (126 suites, 1604 tests) and `make format-check` pass.
* Tests cover every outcome with fakes, including "a pending phone never reads records or opens the vault without the right code".

##### Decisions and problems

* **Partial result:** if the download fails after the vault opened (offline), `restore` answers `partial`, not a failure. The controller unlocks, the mark stays and the next open retries.
* **`restoring` keeps the screen:** the restore screen stays mounted in `pendingDevice` and `restoring`, so the typed code survives an error. The form is replaced by the spinner text.
* **Failed write or open after activation:** the vault is closed and copy A removed, so the phone is a pending-like phone again. Activating twice answers 204, so a retry works.
* **Wipe before write:** a left-over vault file cannot be read with the new key, so `vault.wipe()` runs after the activation and before copy A is written.
* **Field size:** one field of 26 characters in IBM Plex Mono at 15 px, not the six boxes of OnbCorreo.html at 22 px, so it fits the width.
* **Only a phone can check:** the real Argon2id time on a low-end device, the keyboard behaviour of the field, and the restore of a real backup.

##### Next step

Approval from the other phone and "No tengo ninguno de los dos" (SCRUM-63).

#### 2026-10-30 · "No tengo ninguno de los dos": start from zero

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-62. Branch: feature/restore-without-code. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Build the last way out of the restore screen (HU-045): a person with neither the old phone nor the recovery code can start an empty vault after proving it is them with the email code password reset. The server decides (`POST /backup/reset`); the phone explains, calls it, and sets itself up as a first phone.

##### What was done

* **`adapters/api/backupResetApi.ts`:** `reset()` posts with `X-Device-Id` and no body. It maps 204 to ok, 403 `reset_not_allowed` to `notAllowed`, 403 `device_not_active` to `notActive`, a lost connection to `offline`. Contract tests cover each.
* **`application/resetBackup.ts`:** turns the API errors into `ok`, `notAllowed`, `notActive`, `offline` or `failed`.
* **`lockController.ts`:** `resetBackup()` calls it from a pending device and, on `ok`, runs `unlock()`. The phone is now active and the recovery key is gone, so `setUpFirstPhone` makes a new DEK and shows a new recovery code. A controller test checks the whole path.
* **UI:** the option "No tengo ninguno de los dos" opens `ResetExplanation` with the three paragraphs, "Empezar de cero" and "Volver". `notAllowed` shows the message and swaps the primary button for "Restablecer contraseña", which signs out through identity.
* **Tests:** the screen in light and dark, the flow with the controller, the API and the use case.

##### Result

* `make lint-mobile`, `make test-mobile` and `make format-check` pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. This branch is stacked on `feature/restore-with-approval`.

##### Decisions and problems

* **"Empezar de cero" calls the server first**, because the person may have just done the email reset. Only `notAllowed` sends them to the password reset.
* **No direct reset-credentials entry in identity.** `changePassword` needs a session. So "Restablecer contraseña" calls `signOut()`; the person lands on Inicio and Keycloak's login page, which has "¿Olvidaste tu contraseña?". After the reset and a new sign in the phone returns to this screen, and "Empezar de cero" succeeds.
* **The option is shown to pending and active phones**, since both can be stuck without a key.
* **Only a phone can check:** the real Keycloak round trip, the 10 minute window, and that the new session counts as started after the reset.

#### 2026-10-29 · Keep the onboarding progress in the vault and resume it

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-69. Branch: feature/onboarding-progress. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Sofia gave Claude Code the M3 prompt ("You are building module M3 Guided onboarding of Fynk. This module owns the wizard and its navigation only."). Before any code, Claude Code checked `develop` and asked three questions. Sofia chose:

* **4 steps, no Correo.** ADR 0007 verifies the email with a code at sign up, so the email step and `EmailVerificationStep` no longer exist.
* **The header "Omitir" skips only that step.** The warning appears from "Omitir por ahora" and after skipping every step.
* **One name field, like the design.** "take the second word separated by whitespace as the Apellido".

Scope of the session: the wizard shell and the Nombre step. The shell was split into this branch (rules and storage) and `feature/onboarding-wizard-shell` (screens) to keep pull requests small.

##### What was done

* **`domain/steps.ts`:** the four steps `name`, `accounts`, `goals` and `recurring`, with "Paso N de 4", next and previous.
* **`domain/progress.ts`:** pure rules of the setup:
  * `start`, `completeStep` and `skipStep`. `skipStep` reports `showWarning` when the last step is skipped after skipping all the others.
  * `visit` saves the screen on top, after the back button or the back gesture.
  * `skipAll` and `finish`, and `screensUpTo` for the resume stack.
  * A finished setup never moves again.
* **`adapters/vault/migrations.ts`:** table `onboarding_progress` (one row: step, completed and skipped steps as JSON, `finished_at`, `updated_at`).
* **`adapters/vault/vaultProgressStore.ts`:** load and upsert. Step ids this build does not know are dropped, so an old saved setup never breaks the app.
* **`application/onboardingService.ts`:** an external store, ready for React's `useSyncExternalStore`.
  * It reads the progress on `vault.unlocked` and forgets it on `vault.locked`.
  * It saves every move before showing it, and runs one move at a time.
  * It reports `failed` when the vault cannot be read for another reason.
* **Tests:**
  * 27 domain tests.
  * 4 vault contract tests on sql.js.
  * 11 service tests, including "reopens on step 4 after the app is killed on step 4" and "shows the warning before ending when every step was skipped".

##### Result

* Tests: 3 suites, 42 tests pass. ESLint and `tsc` are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet. Stacked on `refactor/share-screen-options`.
* Jira SCRUM-69: the criterion now reads "Pasos: nombre, cuentas y efectivo, metas, gastos recurrentes (el correo se verifica en el registro, ADR 0007)", with a comment explaining the change.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Steps are stored by id, not by number,** so a later change of order does not move a saved setup to the wrong screen.
* **The saved step follows the screen on top** (`visit`). The back gesture can pop a screen without our button, so a separate "back" move would miss it.
* **Finding: the progress is not part of the encrypted backup.** A person who restores on a new phone would see the setup again. The team should decide whether a restore marks it as finished.

##### Next step

The screens of the shell: Aviso, the step frame, Omitir and the gate (`feature/onboarding-wizard-shell`).

#### 2026-10-27 · Consent domain rules (M4 step 1, part 1 of 4)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-20, SCRUM-21. Branch: feature/consents-domain-rules. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

First M4 session. The developer asked to read the whole developer kit first and to treat it as the source of every criterion, decision and procedure. Then: "Let's start by the first branch in the work plan and then we'll work progressively towards completing the whole M4." The first branch is `feature/consents-backend` (schema, endpoints, real `ConsentChecker`, expiry job, audit). It came out to about 2,100 lines, so the developer chose to split it into 4 stacked branches. This is the first one.

##### What was done

* `internal/consents/domain/consent.go`: `Scope`, `Status`, `Text`, `Evidence`, `Consent` and `Request`.
  * `NewPending` checks that the consent is specific (known provider; a non-empty subset of its scopes with no repeats; the purpose and the 365-day term shown on Conectar banco; the text version and the hash of the text the app showed; device id; app version; locale `es-CO`) and stores the acceptance evidence.
  * `Grant` turns a pending consent active only with complete evidence, the same text version and hash, and the bank's consent id. `expires_at = granted_at + term_days`.
  * `StatusAt` and `ActiveAt` report an active consent past its term as expired, even before the daily job writes it.
* `domain/errors.go`: one stable problem code per broken rule (`invalid_scopes`, `purpose_mismatch`, `term_mismatch`, `unknown_text_version`, `text_mismatch`, `missing_evidence` and others).
* Tests from the criteria: what was shown is what is recorded (HU-006), no open or general consents, no activation without evidence and a known text version (module test), and expiry after `term_days` with a fixed time (HU-007 and module test).

##### Result

* Tests: 7 tests (13 rejection cases) pass; `go vet` and golangci-lint are clean.
* Pull request: opened right after this log, based on `chore/add-oapi-codegen-runtime`.

##### Decisions and problems

* The purpose, the 12-month term and locale `es-CO` are constants taken from the M4 prompt. Changing them needs the team (prompt, "Ask before").
* The scopes each provider supports are listed in the domain until M5 exposes its provider list through `bank/ports`. All three providers support the three scopes.
* Go, pnpm (corepack) and the JS dependencies were not installed on this Mac. With the developer's approval they were installed as `docs/local-dev.md` says.

#### 2026-10-27 · Consent use cases, real ConsentChecker and expiry job (M4 step 1, part 3 of 4)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-20, SCRUM-21. Branch: feature/consents-service-and-checker. PR: none. Status: done.

Same session as `feature/consents-domain-rules`. Third of the 4 stacked branches of `feature/consents-backend`.

##### What was done

* `internal/consents/service/service.go`:
  * `Create`: records the explicit acceptance as a pending consent. The same `Idempotency-Key` from the same user returns the first consent; a different request with that key is `idempotency_conflict`.
  * `List` and `Get`: only the user's own consents (another user's is "not found"), newest first, with the status as of now (HU-007, "información actualizada").

  * Audit: one event per state change (`consent.created`, `consent.granted`, `consent.expired`) with `provider` and `consent_id` only, plus the user and device ids.
  * IDs are UUID v7 (shared contracts, section 1).
* `ports/ports.go`: `New(deps)` returns the real checker, so M5 builds it from the `Deps` it already receives, without importing M4 internals. M0's `LocalChecker` stays.
* `store/memory.go`: in-memory store with the same rules, for tests.
* Tests from the criteria: pending with evidence (HU-006), list and status as of now (HU-007), no activation without evidence or a known text (module test), expiry after `term_days` with a fixed clock, audited once (module test), one audit row per change with no amounts, emails or tokens (module test), idempotency, other users hidden.

* `Active` already treats an ended term as inactive, so the daily job only keeps the stored status and the `consent.expired` audit current.

#### 2026-10-28 · Revoke a consent and notify the entity (M4 step 2)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-22, SCRUM-20. Branch: feature/consent-revocation-with-provider. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After M5 step 1, the developer asked to continue with M4 step 2 and whether M4 would be complete after it. Key sentence: "Continue with M4 Step 2. Before doing so, after that is done, is M4 complete or is there anything missing from it?" The answer, given before starting: the backend is complete except for the bank wiring, which comes with M5 step 2's signer. Steps 3 and 4 (mobile) and the Gate C check on staging remain.

##### What was done

* Branch stacked on #46 (M4's endpoints) and #52 (the bank port). Their edits to M0's `app_test.go` conflicted as expected; the merge removes both modules' paths.
* Contract: `POST /consents/{id}/revoke` (`200` revoked, `404`, `409 consent_not_revocable`) and `providerNotifiedAt` on every consent.
* Domain:
  * `Revoke` ends an active or pending consent at once; revoking twice returns it unchanged;
  * an expired or failed consent cannot be revoked;
  * `NoticeFailed` schedules the next try in 10 minutes, and none after 24 hours;
  * `NewPending` now receives the provider's scopes, which the service reads from `bank/ports`.
* Service:
  * `Revoke` stores the revocation, audits `consent.revoked`, then tells the entity right away (5 s limit);
  * `NotifyDue` and `RunNotify` are the retry job, checking every minute;
  * `BankRevoker` reaches M5's installed revoker at call time.
* Store: migration 3 adds `provider_notified_at`, `next_notify_at` and `notify_attempts` to the consent row, plus a check that a revoked consent has its date. `ClaimNotices` uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`.
* `bank/ports` (M5, same developer): `InstallRevoker` and `InstalledRevoker`, the same pattern as `auth.Install`. Until M5 step 2 installs the real revoker, every notice fails with `provider_unavailable` and is retried.
* Tests:
  * the HU-008 criteria;
  * the module test: after revoke, `Active` is false at once and a stubbed M5 query answers `409 consent_revoked`;
  * retries every 10 minutes and stopping after 24 hours;
  * revoking twice notifies once, and a pending consent is revoked without calling the entity;
  * only the owner can revoke;
  * scopes come from `bank/ports`;
  * Postgres tests for revoke, claim and confirm.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass. The 8 store integration tests pass on Postgres 16 in Docker.
* By hand against the real API and Postgres: `cmd/migrate` reaches version 3. Revoking gives `revoked` at once; revoking again gives the same consent; the notice fails (no revoker installed yet) and is scheduled to retry.
* Pull request: opened right after this log.
* Size: about 880 lines, about 430 of them tests.

##### Decisions and problems

* The notice state lives on the consent row, not in a new table. It is consent metadata, which "Where data lives" already keeps in the cloud.
* The first manual run showed the error code as `[REDACTED]`, because M0's logger masks any key named `code` (it treats it as an authorization code). The key is now `error_code`.
* The real notice reaches Banco Demo only after M5 step 2 installs the revoker with the `private_key_jwt` signer.
* Bancolombia consents are refused now, because it is not in M5's provider list until its spike.

##### Next step

M5 step 2: `feature/bank-link-with-par-and-pkce`, which installs the real revoker.

#### 2026-10-29 · Let Jest exit after M4's tests

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-21. Branch: fix/consents-jest-open-handle. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Part of step A that the developer approved ("let's start with A which is quick"). The handoff said Jest prints "a worker process has failed to exit gracefully" on M4 and M5's suites, and asked to "check whether one of our suites leaks a timer".

##### What was done

* Ran each of the 13 M4 and M5 suites alone with `--detectOpenHandles` and a 90 second limit. Twelve exit in 0 to 2 seconds. `consents/application/useConsents.test.tsx` passes but never exits.
* Cause: its test `QueryClient` sets `gcTime: Infinity` only for mutations. After the hooks unmount, each query starts React Query's default 5 minute cleanup timer, which keeps the process alive.
* Fix: `gcTime: Infinity` for queries too, as the other M4 and M5 suites already do. Test code only.

##### Result

* Tests: the suite exits in about 1 second. M4 and M5 together: 13 suites, 130 tests pass, and the worker warning is gone.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Only our suite was checked.** Other modules' suites may have the same pattern. That's for their owners to check, not for us to change.

##### Next step

Open the pull request. Then step B, M5 step 3.

  * the provider catalog.

#### 2026-10-28 · Banco Demo adapter and egress client (M5 step 1, part 2 of 3)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44, SCRUM-45. Branch: feature/bank-demo-adapter. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Continue M5 step 1 after the egress settings PR. Key sentence: "proceed so that we can finish step 1 of the order you propose."

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/egress`: the HTTP client every bank adapter uses. It trusts only `EGRESS_CA_FILE`, needs TLS 1.2 or newer, never follows a redirect (a token can never be sent elsewhere), and times out after 15 s. `BaseURL` builds `<EGRESS_URL>/<route>` and fails when the egress is not configured.
* `internal/bank/adapters/bankdemo`: `ports.Provider` against `contracts/bank-simulator.yaml`, for two providers on one simulator:
  * Banco Demo keeps savings, credit card and CDT; Billetera Demo keeps the wallet. This matches the onboarding design (Banco Demo 3.200.000, Billetera Demo 4.600.000).
  * Accounts map to the shared shape:
    * centavos, with decimal strings converted exactly, never with floats;
    * only the last 4 digits;
    * a card's debt as a positive amount owed (the simulator reports it as negative);
    * the card's next payment date computed in Bogotá time;
    * CDT rate and dates;
    * unknown product types are skipped.
  * Movements map with booked or pending status, merchant and bank category. Pages follow the cursor, 200 a page.
  * OAuth:
    * PAR with PKCE S256, `state`, `nonce`, Fynk's consent id and a `private_key_jwt` assertion; only `client_id` and `request_uri` travel in the authorize URL;
    * code exchange and refresh return the bank's `consent_id`;
    * revocation uses a client credentials token, and a consent the bank no longer knows counts as done.
  * Bank answers become stable errors (`consent_revoked`, `invalid_grant`, `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`, `provider_unavailable`) and never keep the bank's text.
* Tests against a fake server that follows the simulator's contract, plus TLS tests with a second CA.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass.
* Pull request: opened right after this log, stacked on #52 and #53.
* Size: about 1,120 lines, about 490 of them tests.

##### Decisions and problems

* The adapter is not wired into the endpoints yet: they answer `501` until M5 step 2 adds authorizations and links.
* Reading the simulator found more points for M16 besides the three in #52:
  * there is no browser authorization page yet, so the full link flow cannot run end to end;
  * consent ids contain `-`, so the refresh grant reads the wrong consent;
  * the public listener (8443, published as `localhost:8082`) serves the same API and OAuth routes without mTLS;
  * the card debt is negative in `fixtures/demo-user.json` while the shared contract says it is positive.

  They go to the M16 owner in part 3, where the tests against the simulator show them.
* A test first trusted a second server by mistake, because every `httptest` TLS server shares one certificate. The test now gives that server its own certificate.

##### Next step

Part 3: contract tests against the running simulator through Caddy, and the mTLS test with and without the client certificate.

#### 2026-10-29 · Add expo-web-browser for the bank login

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23. Branch: chore/add-expo-web-browser. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer approved step B of the plan: "Yes, push and do B". Its first part is a dependency-only pull request, reviewed by Sofia (M0), because M5 step 3 opens the bank's authorization page in the system browser.

##### What was done

* `apps/mobile/package.json`: `expo-web-browser` `58.0.6`. `pnpm-lock.yaml`: only that package is added (no new transitive dependencies).

* `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and the mobile typecheck pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Version 58.0.6:** the one Expo SDK 58.0.19 bundles (`bundledNativeModules.json` says `~58.0.6`), pinned exactly as ADR 0001 requires. That's how M12 pinned `expo-notifications`.
* **`expo-web-browser`, not `react-native-app-auth`** (already used by M1): our backend returns a ready-made `authorizeUrl` from the PAR, with the PKCE challenge and `state` already inside. The phone has to open that exact URL. `react-native-app-auth` builds its own authorization URL, with its own `state` and PKCE. `openAuthSessionAsync` opens our URL as is, in the same system browser session (ASWebAuthenticationSession on iOS, Custom Tabs on Android), so it's still never a webview.
* **No `app.json` change:** the package's config plugin only does something with the optional `experimentalLauncherActivity` setting, which we don't use.
* **A new dev build is needed** once code uses the native module. Sofia's call when to rebuild.

`feature/bank-vault-tables`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The bank API adapter, PKCE and the callback check on the phone

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-link-api-and-pkce. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Third part of step B ("Yes, push and do B"): the phone's adapter for the bank endpoints, with tokens only in bodies and a new `X-Request-Nonce` on every call, plus pure helpers for the verifier, the S256 challenge and the state.

##### What was done

* `domain/pkce.ts`:
  * `base64Url`;
  * `challengeOf` (SHA-256 from `@noble/hashes`, as M2's domain already uses);
  * `createPkce(random)`: a 43 character verifier from 32 random bytes, and a state from 16;
  * `newNonce(random)`: 32 characters.
  * The random bytes are injected, so the domain stays pure.
* `domain/callback.ts`: `BANK_CALLBACK_URL` (`fynk://bank/callback`) and `readCallback(url, expectedState)`. It returns:
  * `code`;
  * `denied` (`access_denied`);
  * `failed` (other bank errors);
  * `state_mismatch`;
  * `invalid` (another URL, or no code or error).
  * The state is checked before the code or the error is trusted. It parses the query by hand, because React Native's `URL` has no `searchParams`.
* `adapters/bankApi.ts` with the `BankApiPort` port: `authorize`, `exchange`, `accounts`, `transactions` (cursor) and `refresh`.
* Tests:
  * PKCE against the RFC 7636 Appendix B vector, plus unique values and lengths;
  * 6 callback cases;
  * the adapter on a fake fetch: request bodies, no token in any URL, a new nonce per call, and `consent_revoked` coming through as `ApiError.code`.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 10 suites, 52 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 475 lines, more than half tests.
* Nothing calls the adapter yet. Step B4 wires it to Conectar banco.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Contract mismatch in our own module:** `contracts/modules/bank.yaml` says the nonce is 16 to 64 characters, and the backend (`service/queries.go`) accepts 16 to 128. The phone sends 32, which fits both. To fix later in a contract PR of ours: change `maxLength` to 128, or the backend to 64.
* **A mistake in the first test,** caught by the test itself: the RFC verifier string was typed wrong. Node's `crypto` confirmed that the code and the RFC's octets and challenge agree, and the test was fixed.
* **The verifier and state stay in memory** during the browser handoff (B4), never in the vault or a log.

##### Next step

`feature/mobile-connect-bank-flow`.

#### 2026-10-29 · Consent-checked bank queries, refresh, nonces and rate limit (M5 step 2, part 8)

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44, SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/bank-link-queries. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Same session as `2026-10-29-juanleal05-m5-bank-link-settings.md`. Key sentence: "Then let's continue with M5 step 2 as better as possible." The developer also asked to look at the CI error of #131.

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/service/queries.go`: before every bank call, a trusted device, 30 queries a minute per user, the link of that user and device, and the consent still active. When the consent ended (in Fynk or at the bank) the link ends and the answer is 409 `consent_revoked`.
  * `Accounts` audits `bank.sync` with the accounts count; failures audit `bank.sync_failed` with the error code.
  * `Refresh` needs the link's current refresh token (its fingerprint), then rotates it; `bank.token_refreshed`.
  * The bank's own 429 is passed back with its `Retry-After`.
* `server.go` and `module.go`: the query and refresh endpoints, 429 with `Retry-After`, and a `X-Request-Nonce` check on every `POST /bank/*` (400 `nonce_required`, 409 `replayed_request`).
* Tests: a refresh token of another link is rejected, the old token stops working after a refresh, a revoked consent answers 409 and ends the link, another user or device gets `link_not_found`, the 31st query gets 429, a replayed nonce is rejected; and the same over HTTP.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass on the whole stack. The Postgres and Redis tests pass against Postgres 16 and Redis 7 in Docker.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The #131 CI "failure" was not a code problem.** `gh stack submit` starts two CI runs for the same commit when it opens a pull request. The workflow's concurrency rule cancels one, and its `CI passed` job fails on purpose for a cancelled run. The other run passed, and it is the one that counts. It can be avoided in M0's workflow (for example, skip `CI passed` when the run itself was cancelled). That is Sofia's call, so nothing was changed.
* **`link_id` is not in M13's allow-list for `bank.sync_failed`,** so that event carries only the provider, the error code and the status.
* **Banco Demo has no authorization page yet,** so the whole link cannot run end to end. Everything is tested against a fake provider and the stores against real Postgres and Redis.

##### Next step

Review and merge this stack bottom-up. Then M5 step 3 (the phone opens the portal and keeps the tokens), once M2's vault and M16's authorization page exist; and the Bancolombia spike.

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-link-signer. PR: none. Status: done.

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/keys`:
  * `LocalSigner`: the `private_key_jwt` assertion, PS256, with iss and sub the client id, aud the bank, a random jti and exp 60 s. Reads PKCS #1 or PKCS #8 PEM and refuses keys under 2048 bits.
  * `LocalHMAC`: the HMAC-SHA-256 fingerprint of a refresh token, with a key of at least 32 bytes; `Matches` compares in constant time.
  * Both sit behind the interfaces the KMS keys will use in AWS.
* Tests: claims and lifetime, a new jti every time, not accepted as RS256 or HS256, weak keys, both PEM formats, fingerprints per token and per key.

##### Result

* Tests: `make lint` and `make test` pass on the whole stack. The Postgres and Redis tests pass against Postgres 16 and Redis 7 in Docker.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

* **`golang-jwt/jwt/v5` was already a dependency,** so no library was added.
* **The first commit had the signer and the links table together (about 830 lines).** It was split in two before pushing. Renaming the branch broke `gh stack`'s local record, which was fixed by renaming the entry in `.git/gh-stack` (a backup was kept).

#### 2026-10-29 · The link flow on the phone, through the system browser

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-23, SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/mobile-connect-bank-flow. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Fourth part of step B ("Yes, push and do B"): after "Ir al portal", create the authorization, open the bank in the system browser, check `state`, exchange the code, save the tokens in the vault, then read the accounts, with clear errors for every case.

##### What was done

* `application/linkBank.ts`: `linkBank(deps, { provider, consentId })`:
  * creates PKCE and the state, then `POST /bank/authorizations`;
  * opens the `authorizeUrl` as is, and checks the return with `readCallback`;
  * exchanges the code with the verifier and saves the link and tokens in one vault transaction, with the expiry from `expiresIn` and the clock;
  * then `POST /bank/accounts/query`.
* Outcomes:
  * `linked` (with the accounts, an empty list, or `null` if only the accounts query failed);
  * `cancelled` (the browser was closed);
  * `denied`;
  * `failed` with a stable reason: `state_mismatch`, `bank_error`, `expired` (`authorization_not_found`), `already_linked`, `unavailable`, `offline`, `device_required` or `unknown`.
* `adapters/systemBrowser.ts`: `openAuthSessionAsync` with `preferEphemeralSession`, and `secureRandom` from `expo-crypto`.
* Tests:
  * 13 for the flow on a real SQLite vault: the verifier sent at the exchange matches the challenge sent first; a wrong state exchanges nothing; nothing is stored when any step fails; the link stays if only the accounts query fails; every error code maps to its reason;
  * 6 for the browser adapter.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 12 suites, 71 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 340 lines.
* Not yet on screen: the next layer wires it to Conectar banco and shows the result.

##### Decisions and problems

* **The screen comes in its own layer,** so this PR stays near 340 lines and the next one is only UI.
* **Ephemeral browser session:** the bank's cookies aren't kept after the link (iOS). The person logs in at the bank every time they link. That's the safer default for a bank login.
* **The accounts shown right after linking aren't stored.** M6's `upsertFromSync` isn't merged, and storing accounts is the sync's job (step 4, first sync of 12 months).
* **Open risk:** if the exchange works but the vault write fails (for example, the vault locked at that moment), the backend has an active link the phone has no token for. The person sees `unknown` and can revoke the consent and link again. A retry isn't possible: the code is single-use.
* **Only a phone can check:** that Android's Custom Tab return reaches `openAuthSessionAsync`, and that React Navigation ignores `fynk://bank/callback` (no screen has that path).
* **End to end against Banco Demo isn't possible yet:** the simulator has no authorization page (M16, Juan Pablo).

##### Next step

`feature/connect-bank-result-screen`: the screen states, `ConnectBankButton` and `BankLinkList`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The phone's sync engine

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-24, SCRUM-25, SCRUM-22. Branch: feature/mobile-sync-engine. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second layer of step C ("After that, start with C"): for each active link, refresh if needed, read the accounts and each account's movements by cursor, write them through M6 and M7 with no duplicates on the 3-day overlap, emit `sync.completed` or `sync.failed`, and end the link on `409 consent_revoked`.

##### What was done

* `application/syncEngine.ts`: `createSyncEngine(deps).syncAll()`. For each active link:
  * the 60 s cooldown, then `markAttempt`;
  * renew the access token if under 60 s are left (the new tokens are kept);
  * `POST /bank/accounts/query`, then each account's movements by cursor from `syncSince`;
  * accounts to M6 through `AccountsSink`, movements to M7 through `TransactionsSink`;
  * `markSynced`.
* Results per link: `synced`, `cooldown`, `revoked` or `failed`, with the API's code.
  * `consent_revoked` and `link_not_found` end the link on the phone: revoked, tokens deleted, M6's `markLinkRevoked`.
  * `rate_limited` stops asking for the remaining links.
  * Calls made during a run share it. A locked vault (`VaultLockedError`) does nothing.
* Events: `sync.failed` per failed or revoked link, and one `sync.completed` with the synced link ids, the new movement ids and the changed account ids.
* `application/ports.ts`: `AccountsSink` (M6's shape) and `TransactionsSink` (the writer M5 needs from M7). `LinkStore` gets `markAttempt` and `markSynced`.
* Tests: 12 for the engine on a real SQLite vault with fakes for the API, M6 and M7, plus 1 for the store marks. They cover:
  * 12 months first;
  * two pulls within 60 s make one network call;
  * a shared run;
  * the 3-day overlap gives no new ids;
  * cursor paging;
  * token renewal;
  * revocation (both codes);
  * a revoked link is never synced;
  * a failure keeps the last update date;
  * `rate_limited`;
  * a locked vault.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 16 suites, 113 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 500 lines, about 260 of them tests. Over the 400 guideline; the engine and its tests are kept together.
* Not wired yet: nothing calls the engine. The next layer adds the triggers.

##### Decisions and problems

* **M7 has no writer for synced movements.** `TransactionsSink` is the shape M5 needs: upsert by `(accountId, providerTxId)`, delete the pending movements since `since` that the bank no longer returns, and return the new ids. Sofia (M7) decides whether M7 offers this or something else.
* **`refresh_token_mismatch` is a failure, not a revocation:** the link stays, and the person sees the error. If it keeps happening, they revoke and link again.
* **The movement count isn't audited on the backend** (`bank.sync` has `accounts_count` only). The phone knows the count, but the backend only sees pages. Left as it is.
* **The movements of every account type are asked for, CDTs too.** If Banco Demo answers with an error for a CDT, that link fails; to watch once the simulator runs end to end.

##### Next step

`feature/sync-triggers-and-feedback`.

#### 2026-10-29 · The sync rules and the mapping to Account and Transaction

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-24, SCRUM-25. Branch: feature/sync-rules. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer asked to push step 3 and "After that, start with C" (M5 step 4, the sync engine). First layer: the pure rules, with an injected clock.

##### What was done

* `domain/syncRules.ts`:
  * `canStartSync`: 60 s per link, counted from the last start; a clock that went back never locks a link;
  * `shouldSyncOnForeground`: 15 minutes or more in the background;
  * `syncSince`: 12 calendar months back on the first sync (29 February goes to 28 February), else 3 days before the last sync;
  * `needsRefresh`: less than 60 s left on the access token.
* `domain/syncMapping.ts`:
  * `toAccount` and `toTransaction` map the bank's shapes to the shared `Account` and `Transaction` (contracts, section 2);
  * `accountIdOf` and `transactionIdOf` derive stable ids from the bank's own ids (SHA-256, length-prefixed parts);
  * movements get `merchantKey` with `normalizeMerchant`, and stay as `other` with `categorySource: 'rule'` for M7;
  * an unknown provider is refused.
* Tests: 10 for the rules, 7 for the mapping.

##### Result

* Tests: bank 15 suites, 100 tests. Typecheck and ESLint pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 330 lines.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Stable ids:** M6's `upsertFromSync` keeps the id of the first row it sees for each `(provider, providerAccountId)`. If M5 made a new id on each sync, movements would point to an account id M6 never stored. Deriving the id from the bank's ids avoids that, and gives M7 the same movement id on the 3-day overlap.
* **"After 15 minutes" means 15 minutes in the background.** The prompt says "AppState back to active after 15 minutes", which could also mean since the last sync. The 60 s cooldown still applies on top. To confirm with the team if they meant otherwise.
* **12 months are calendar months,** not 365 days.

##### Next step

`feature/mobile-sync-engine`.

#### 2026-10-30 · The KMS client assertion signer and token fingerprint

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-kms-keys. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

The developer asked to start step D: "Start with D, but for this part, use a different stack if possible so that we don't have a stack of more than 10 PRs". First layer of a new stack from `develop`: the KMS signer and fingerprint behind the interfaces M5 already has.

##### What was done

* `internal/bank/keys/kms.go`:
  * a small `KMS` interface (`SignDigest`, `GenerateMac`);
  * `KMSSigner`: builds the same claims as the local signer, sends KMS only the SHA-256 digest of `header.payload` (RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256), and appends the signature;
  * `KMSFingerprinter`: KMS `GenerateMac` with an HMAC_256 key; its errors never contain the token.
* `signer.go`: the claims (iss, sub, aud, random jti, exp 60 s) move to `assertionClaims`, shared by both signers. `LocalSigner` behaves the same.
* `kms_test.go`: a fake KMS with local keys (PSS with the salt equal to the hash, as KMS does, and HMAC-SHA-256). It checks:
  * the assertion verifies as PS256 with the public key of an RSA 3072 key (M14's spec);
  * KMS gets only the digest of the signing input, with the right key id;
  * every jti differs;
  * missing values are refused and KMS errors come through;
  * the fingerprint equals HMAC-SHA-256 of the token;
  * no error contains the token.

##### Result

* Tests: `go test ./internal/bank/...` passes. gofmt, go vet and golangci-lint (0 issues) pass.
* Pull request: not opened yet. About 260 lines.
* Not used yet: the AWS SDK adapter and the settings come in the next layers, and they need the developer's OK (`go.mod` and `platform/config` are M0's).

##### Decisions and problems

* **A new stack,** as the developer asked, so stack #188 stays at 9 PRs.
* **No `kid` header,** as with the local signer. If Banco Demo or a real bank needs one to find Fynk's key, it's one line in `assertionClaims`' caller.
* **Switching to KMS changes every fingerprint,** because the KMS HMAC key isn't the local file key. Links made with the local key won't refresh under KMS. Staging has no links yet (no bank settings), so nothing breaks there.

##### Next step

Ask the developer to approve `chore/add-aws-kms-sdk` (`go.mod`) and the KMS settings in `platform/config`, both reviewed by Sofia.

#### 2026-10-30 · Use the KMS keys for the bank link in AWS

Juan Carlos Leal (JuanLeal05) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-44. Branch: feature/bank-kms-wiring. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Last layer of the KMS stack, approved by the developer: "Yes, do it and create the stack with the PRs, Sofia and Juan Pablo must be called to review either way". The SDK adapter, the KMS key settings in `platform/config` (M0, Sofia reviews) and the choice of key in the bank wiring.

##### What was done

* `internal/platform/config` (M0): two optional settings, `BANK_SIGNER_KMS_KEY_ID` and `BANK_FINGERPRINT_KMS_KEY_ID` (id, ARN or alias). Nothing is required, so every environment keeps starting. One test reads them and the "link off" test checks they default to empty.
* `internal/bank/keys/kmsclient.go`: `AWSKMS` over the SDK, `Sign` with `MessageType DIGEST` and `RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256`, and `GenerateMac` with `HMAC_SHA_256`. An empty answer is an error.
* `internal/bank/wiring.go`: `bankKeys` takes each key from KMS when its id is set, else from its file. The AWS client is built once, from the default chain (`AWS_REGION`, the ECS task role). With neither source, or if the AWS client can't load, the link stays off and the revoker stays unavailable, as before.
* Tests:
  * the adapter against a fake KMS endpoint: checks the `X-Amz-Target` operation and the JSON fields (key id, `DIGEST`, `RSASSA_PSS_SHA_256`, `HMAC_SHA_256`, base64 message), errors, and empty answers;
  * the wiring: KMS ids alone build the providers with the KMS fingerprinter and one client; local files never load AWS; an AWS client that can't load keeps the link off.

##### Result

* Pull request: not opened yet. About 300 lines.

#### 2026-10-29 · Accounts in the vault, the write API for M5 and the read API

Sofia Nicolle Ariza (sofia-ariza-783) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-26, SCRUM-27, SCRUM-28. Branch: feature/accounts-vault-and-sync-api. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Second part of the first M6 work plan step: "tables, write API for M5". Same session as `2026-10-29-sofia-ariza-783-m6-net-worth.md`, split in its own stacked branch to keep each pull request small.

##### What was done

* `adapters/vault/migrations.ts`: `accounts_accounts` with every `Account` field, a unique index on `(provider, provider_account_id)` (no duplicates), a unique index that allows a single cash account, and `accounts_balance_history (account_id, on_date, balance_minor)`.
* `adapters/vault/vaultAccountsStore.ts`: upsert by `(provider, provider_account_id)` that keeps the first id, so an account linked again keeps its history; one balance point per account per day in Bogotá, the last sync of the day wins.
* `application/accountsService.ts`: `upsertFromSync`, `markLinkRevoked`, `getOverview`, `getAccounts`, `getBalanceTotal`, `getConnectedAccountsSummary`, `getCdtAccounts`, `getBalanceHistory`, and a change listener.

* `index.ts`: registers the vault migrations, listens to `sync.completed`, `vault.unlocked` and `vault.locked`, and exports the write and read API. The Dashboard tab is still M0's placeholder.
* 14 tests on a real SQLite vault (sql.js) and on the hook.

##### Result

* Tests: 45 of 45 in the module, 1087 of 1087 in the app. Lint and typecheck clean.
* Pull request: second in the M6 stack.

##### Decisions and problems

* A whole sync is validated before anything is written, so a malformed account never leaves half a sync in the vault.
* Revoked accounts stay in the vault with status `revoked` and leave every list and total. A new link to the same bank brings them back with the same id.
* The query has no time based staleness: data changes only through sync, unlock, lock or a write, which all notify the hook (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

##### Next step

Cash account, its history, the `accounts.cash` backup collection and `CashInput`.

#### 2026-10-29 · Contributions to savings goals: manual record, linked movement and undo

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-52. Branch: feature/savings-contributions. PR: none. Status: partial.

##### Instruction

After the Metas screen (#173) was opened, Claude offered the Dashboard carousel or SCRUM-52. Karol answered "con scrom 52". The story is HU-036 Registrar aportes: "Asociar un movimiento sincronizado. Aporte manual con monto y fecha. Fynk no ejecuta transferencias. Se refleja en movimientos como aporte a plan de ahorro. Se puede deshacer."

##### What was done

M7 (movements) is still M0's placeholder and #173 was still open, so SCRUM-52 is split. This pull request holds the rules and the service, on `develop` and without touching the files of #173. The "Abonar" part of the Metas card comes after #173 is merged.

* **`domain/contribution.ts`:**
  * `validateContribution`: amount above $0 and up to $1.000.000.000, a real day that is not in the future, note of 80 characters or less.
  * `contributionFromMovement`: only a booked outflow that is not linked yet; the amount is the money that left, on its day in Bogotá.
* **`application/contributionService.ts`:**
  * `add`: a manual record.
  * `linkMovement`: creates the contribution and marks the movement in M7 with `setTransactionGoal`. If M7 fails, the contribution is undone.
  * `undo`: marks the row deleted and frees the movement.
  * `unlinkGoal`.
  * Errors: invalid input, goal not found, movement not eligible, movement already linked, movements unavailable.
* **`application/contributionPorts.ts`:** `ContributionStore`, and `MovementGoalLinker` (the shape of M7's `setTransactionGoal`).
* **`adapters/vault/vaultContributionStore.ts`:** on the existing `savings_contributions` table. A movement already linked is rejected before the unique index is reached.

#### 2026-10-28 · Detection of recurring payments (M9 domain)

Karol (Karol2905) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-55. Branch: feature/recurring-detection. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

After #95 was merged, Karol asked to go on: "listo, sigue". Claude had proposed the detection algorithm as the next M9 step because it is pure and can be tested on the shared fixture.

##### What was done

* `modules/recurring/domain/detect.ts`: `detectRecurring`, steps 1 to 9 of the M9 prompt. It groups booked outflows by `merchantKey` and keeps groups with 3 charges, or 2 when the catalog says the merchant is a subscription. It classifies the rhythm by the median interval: daily on the weekdays seen, weekly on the most common weekday, biweekly, monthly on the most common day of the month, or yearly. Amounts must be within ± 10 % of the median, or ± 35 % for utilities and telecom. The score is 0,45 for regularity, 0,45 for amount stability and 0,1 for a catalog match, and a group is proposed at 0,7 or more. Each proposal has a kind, the amount of the last charge, the next charge, the account or card of the last charge and the movements behind it. A dismissed proposal returns only after an amount change of more than 30 %.
* `detect.test.ts`: 16 tests. On `fixtures/demo-user.json` it finds the 8 seeded recurring payments: rent on day 1, utilities with a variable amount, the gym every Monday, transport Monday to Friday, and 4 subscriptions that add up to $112.600. It flags none of café, restaurants, supermarket, the 3 big purchases, or moves between own accounts. Hand-made cases cover the 2-charge catalog rule, ± 10 % against ± 35 %, the 31st in a 30 day month, the latest amount, a stopped payment, dismissed proposals, biweekly charges and Bogotá calendar days.

##### Result

* Tests: 41 in the module pass (16 new). ESLint, `tsc` and Prettier are clean.
* Pull request: not opened yet.

##### Decisions and problems

* Transfers, card payments, savings and income are never proposed: they move the person's own money and would count it twice. The M9 prompt does not say this; it follows from what a recurring expense is.
* A payment with no charge for two periods (for example 65 days for a monthly one) is not proposed, so a service the person already cancelled does not come back.
* The catalog is passed as a lookup function. The real one comes from `GET /catalog/merchants` when the catalog backend is built (it needs Go 1.27, not available in this session).

##### Next step

Open the pull request. Then `feature/recurring-items-manual` when M2's vault is in `develop`, or the catalog backend where Go 1.27 is installed.

* **`recurring/domain/reminder.ts`:** `reminderPlan` finds the next charge whose reminder is still ahead: lead days before (0, 1 or 2) at 9:00 Bogotá (14:00 UTC). It uses the existing `nextCharge`, so the 31st becomes the 30th in a 30 day month. Fixed expenses give `payment_due`; subscriptions give `subscription_renewal`.
* **`recurring/application/reminders.ts`:** schedules the reminder through M12's `NotificationsPort`, or cancels it.
  * Each item has one id, `recurring.reminder.<id>`, so scheduling again replaces it.
  * The dedupe key includes the charge date.
  * The link is `fynk://billetera/recurrentes/<id>`.
  * Texts are in `strings.es-CO.ts`: "Vence en 2 días: $186.000." and "Se renueva el 28 sep por $38.900.".
* **`recurringService`:**
  * create and update schedule the reminder.
  * delete cancels it.
  * New `refreshReminders()` reschedules every item.
  * A failing notifier never blocks saving.
* **`recurring/index.ts`:**
  * The reminders refresh on `vault.unlocked`, on `sync.completed` and when Ajustes de notificaciones changes.
  * New route `recurring.Edit` with path `billetera/recurrentes/:id`, so the reminder's link opens the item in the form.
* **M12 (also Karol's):** the service has `onSettingsChanged`, exported as `onNotificationSettingsChanged`. It fires when a kind, "Permitir notificaciones" or the lead days change.
* **Tests:**
  * `domain/reminder.test.ts` (8): 2 days before at 9:00, chosen lead days, 31st in a 30 day month, reminder already passed, year change, weekly, renewal, nothing when off, cancelled or daily.
  * `application/reminders.test.ts` (9): scheduling on create, renewal text, lead days, moving and turning off, delete, refresh, a failing notifier, the texts.
  * `notifications/application/settings.test.ts` (+1).

##### Result

* Tests: 79 suites and 1042 tests pass. ESLint, tsc and Prettier are clean.
* Checked that `billetera/recurrentes/<id>` resolves to `recurring.Edit` with the id, and that `billetera/recurrentes/nuevo` still opens the new item form.
* Pull request: stacked on the rescue pull request.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Daily items get no reminder.** A notice every weekday for transport would only be noise. The switch still shows in the form; the team can change this.
* **When the reminder of the next charge already passed** (an item added the day before it is charged), the reminder is for the charge after, so no reminder is ever sent late with the wrong "Vence en 2 días".
* **No background work.** Reminders are rescheduled only with the app open; the phone rings the local notification that was already scheduled.
* **Merging the stack.** It reached the wrong branches because the upper pull requests were merged without first changing their base to `develop`. Next time: merge the bottom one, delete its branch (GitHub then moves the next base to `develop`), click "Update branch", and only then merge.

##### Next step

SCRUM-56: usage marks, the subscriptions section and the savings banner in Billetera.

#### 2026-10-28 · POST /recommendations: Bedrock with post checks and fallback

Julián Castiblanco (JulCR34l) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-38, SCRUM-39, SCRUM-40. Branch: feature/recommendations-endpoint. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Julián asked Claude (claude.ai, Fynk project, session linked to the repository zip): "soy la persona 6, que hace la parte de analytics AI and investment, ayúdame a realizarlo con la información que hay en el repo y con respecto al módulo". Claude followed `docs/modules/M10-analytics.md`, `M11-recommendations.md` and `M15-investment.md`, `00-shared-contracts.md` and the design sources, and built the three modules as one stack of branches.

##### What was done

* `contracts/modules/recommendations.yaml`: `POST /recommendations` with the snapshot schema (no extra fields, 12 months, enums), `400 invalid_snapshot`, `429 rate_limited`. The example health path is removed.
* `internal/recommendations/advisor`: `Decode` (16 KB limit, unknown fields rejected anywhere, ranges and enums), `Check` (known and unique factKeys, every `$` amount and percent present in or derived from the snapshot, no product, issuer, bank, ticker or promise of returns, length limits), `Service.Generate` (one call, one retry, then `fallback: true`; logs only hash, model, tokens and latency).
* `internal/recommendations/prompt/system.es.md`: versioned Spanish system prompt.
* `internal/recommendations/bedrock`: Converse adapter with a cache point after the system prompt and the answer forced through the `write_recommendations` tool. Temperature 0.2, 800 tokens.
* `ratelimit.go`: 10 requests per person per Bogotá day in Redis.
* `internal/app/app_test.go` (M0's file): no longer expects recommendations to be a stub.
* Golden tests on 5 fixture snapshots × 11 kinds of bad output, plus decode, HTTP, Bedrock adapter and settings tests.

##### Result

* `go vet` and `go test -race` pass for the module and for `internal/app` (run with Go 1.25 against the generated server, see problems).
* Not tested against the real Bedrock yet (manual staging run of the module prompt: 20 runs, same numbers).

##### Decisions and problems

* The response carries `modelId`, `promptVersion` and `snapshotHash` (RN-04 traceability).
* The snapshot guard runs as middleware before the generated server, because the generated types silently drop unknown fields.
* Settings come from the module's own environment variables (`RECOMMENDATIONS_MODEL_ID`, `RECOMMENDATIONS_AWS_REGION`, `RECOMMENDATIONS_DAILY_LIMIT`); M0 may prefer to move them into `platform/config` and `.env.example`.
* The build environment had only Go 1.24/1.25 and no access to proxy.golang.org. The code was compiled and tested with Go 1.25, the same oapi-codegen v2.8.1 and dependencies fetched from GitHub mirrors. CI with Go 1.27 is the real check.
* The app test change touches M0's file: needs Sofía's approval.

##### Next step

* Staging: set the Bedrock model access and run the 20-run manual check of Gate E.
