#### 2026-10-28 · M13 Step 1: Append-only hash-chained audit store and verification

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-41. Branch: feature/audit-store-append-only. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Continue with the last assigned module (M13 Compliance, privacy and audit), starting with Phase 1: `feature/audit-store-append-only` (HU-027, SCRUM-41).

##### What was done

* Database Migrations (`services/api/internal/audit/migrations/`):
  * `1_create_events.up.sql`: Created `events` table in schema `audit` with fields `id`, `type`, `user_id`, `device_id`, `at`, `meta`, `prev_hash`, `hash`, and indexes on `user_id` and `at`.
  * Implemented `prevent_event_modification()` trigger function and triggers `trg_prevent_event_modification` (BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE) and `trg_prevent_event_truncate` (BEFORE TRUNCATE) enforcing database immutability.
  * Embedded migration files into `migrations.FS`.
* Sensitive Data Rejecter & Allow-list Validation (`services/api/internal/audit/validation.go`):
  * Strictly validated `ports.Event` against recognized event types (`ports.AuthLogin` through `ports.PrivacyDeletionCompleted`).
  * Enforced sensitive key detection rejecting forbidden terms (`amount`, `email`, `token`, `account_number`, `password`, `cedula`, `authorization`, `card`, `pan`, `cvv`, `jwt`, `secret`).
  * Validated `device_id` as valid UUID (when present) and metadata keys against type-specific allow-lists.
* Canonical Serialization & Hash Chaining (`services/api/internal/audit/canonical.go`):
  * Constructed deterministic canonical JSON encoding `CanonicalRow` with sorted map keys and microsecond-precision UTC timestamps (`2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z`).
  * Computed SHA-256 hash chaining: `hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical_json_of_row)` with a 32-byte zero slice for genesis row.
* PostgreSQL Audit Store (`services/api/internal/audit/store.go`):
  * Implemented `Store` implementing `ports.Recorder`.
  * `Record`: acquires PostgreSQL transaction advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`) to serialize append operations, fetches latest row hash as `prev_hash`, computes canonical hash, and inserts row.
  * `Verify`: scans rows ordered by `id ASC`, checks chain continuity (`prev_hash == previous_row.hash`), reconstructs canonical representation, and recalculates SHA-256 hashes to detect tampering or row removal.
* Verification Endpoint & CLI Command:
  * Added `GET /internal/audit/verify` handler in `services/api/internal/audit/http.go` and mounted on `app.NewRouter` with `auth.Require` and `app.WithAuditVerifier`.
  * Added CLI command `fynk-api audit verify` in `services/api/cmd/api/main.go`.
* Architectural Decision Record (`docs/adr/0004-audit-retention.md`):
  * Documented retention period proposal: 5 years for consent and privacy events (under Ley 1581 and statutory limitations for commercial claims) and 1 year for high-volume operational events.
* Tests:
  * Unit tests (`validation_test.go`, `canonical_test.go`, `http_test.go`, `app_test.go`).
  * Integration tests (`store_integration_test.go`) validating that `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, and `TRUNCATE` fail due to trigger, and verifying tamper/deletion detection.

##### Result

* `make lint-go` passed with 0 issues.
* `make format-check` passed.
* `make test-go test-scripts` passed.

##### Decisions and problems

* Advisory transaction lock `pg_advisory_xact_lock(8472918471928471)` guarantees a strictly linear hash chain across concurrent API instances without table-level deadlocks.
* Truncating timestamps to microsecond precision before computing hashes avoids floating point/nanosecond discrepancies when reading back from PostgreSQL `timestamptz`.

##### Next step

Step 2: `feature/keycloak-events-import` (Phase 1, importing Keycloak user events into the audit trail every 5 minutes).

#### 2026-11-01 · Cascade account deletion under Ley 1581 de 2012 (SCRUM-42)

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Antigravity. Jira: SCRUM-42. Branch: feature/account-deletion. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Implement the cascade account deletion endpoints for Module M13 per `docs/modules/M13-compliance.md` and Jira SCRUM-42 (HU-028):
1. `POST /privacy/deletion-requests`: requires authentication and fresh login in the last 5 minutes (`user.SessionID` verified via `identity.Sessions`). Executes cascade deletion in legal sequence:
   * Step 1: Revoke every consent through M4 (notifies bank provider).
   * Step 2: Mark every bank link revoked and delete bank link records.
   * Step 3: Delete backup records, recovery key, and device approvals.
   * Step 4: Delete registered devices.
   * Step 5: Delete Keycloak user via Keycloak admin API.
   * Step 6: Retain only deletion evidence (`privacy.deletion_requests`: request id, user id hash, timestamps, steps done) and immutable audit records.
2. `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}`: public endpoint returning completion status and legal confirmation message: `"Tu cuenta y tus datos fueron eliminados el <fecha>."`.
3. Unit and integration tests verifying zero rows remain for the deleted user across `identity`, `backup`, `bank`, and only revoked evidence in `consents`.

##### What was done

* **OpenAPI Contract (`contracts/modules/privacy.yaml`):**
  * Added `POST /privacy/deletion-requests` with optional `PrivacyDeletionRequestCreate` body (typed confirmation `ELIMINAR` and optional reason).
  * Added `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` with `security: []` (public) returning `PrivacyDeletionRequest`.
  * Updated schemas: `PrivacyDeletionRequest` (`id`, `status`, `createdAt`, `completedAt`, `stepsDone`, `message`) and `PrivacyDeletionRequestCreate`.
  * Ran `make generate` to update Go server interface and mobile TypeScript definitions.
* **Database Migration (`services/api/internal/privacy/migrations/`):**
  * Created `1_create_deletion_requests.up.sql` creating `privacy.deletion_requests` with `user_id_hash bytea NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id_hash) = 32)` and index on `user_id_hash`.
  * Created `1_create_deletion_requests.down.sql` dropping table and index.
  * Embedded migration files via `migrations.FS`.
* **Module Ports (`identity/ports` and `consents/ports`):**
  * `identity/ports`: added `UserDeleter` interface and `NewUserDeleter(cfg)` delegating to `kcadmin.Client.DeleteUser`.
  * `consents/ports`: added `ConsentsRevoker` interface and `NewConsentsRevoker(deps)` to safely revoke active and pending user consents with bank notification across module boundaries.
* **Privacy Module Implementation (`services/api/internal/privacy/`):**
  * `deleter.go`: implemented `Deleter` with `DeleteAccount` and `GetRequest`:
    * Fresh session verification against `identity.Sessions` within 5 minutes (returning `fresh_login_required` 403 on stale or missing session).
    * Confirmation validation (returning `invalid_confirmation` 400 if confirmation keyword mismatch).
    * Cascade deletion across consents, bank links, backup records, recovery keys, device approvals, identity devices, and Keycloak user.
    * Record audit events: `privacy.deletion_requested` and `privacy.deletion_completed`.
    * Record deletion evidence in `privacy.deletion_requests` storing only `sha256(user_id)` to preserve Zero-Knowledge privacy.
    * Formatted legal completion message in Spanish (`DeletionMessage`).
  * `server.go`: implemented `CreateDeletionRequest` (returning 202 Accepted) and `GetDeletionRequest` (returning 200 OK or 404).
  * `module.go`: wired `deleter`, returned `migrations.FS` in `Migrations()`, and allowed public access to `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` without authentication token.
* **Tests:**
  * Updated `services/api/internal/privacy/privacy_test.go` with unit tests for `POST /v1/privacy/deletion-requests` (unauthenticated, bad confirmation, success 202), public `GET /v1/privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` (404/200 without token), and fresh login verification (stale vs fresh session).
  * Added `deleter_integration_test.go` testing against PostgreSQL and asserting zero rows remain in `identity.devices`, `bank.links`, `backup.records`, `backup.recovery_keys`, and `backup.device_approvals`, while retaining revoked evidence in `consents.consents` and hashed evidence in `privacy.deletion_requests`.
  * Updated `services/api/internal/app/app_test.go` stub path list.

##### Result

* `make lint-go`: clean (0 issues).
* `make test-go test-scripts`: clean (100% passed).
* `make format-check`: clean.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Public status lookup after account deletion:** After an account is deleted, the user's Keycloak account and sessions are destroyed, meaning they cannot provide an Authorization token. Marked `GET /privacy/deletion-requests/{id}` with `security: []` in the OpenAPI specification and exempt in `requireAuth` middleware so users can check confirmation after account deletion.
* **Zero-Knowledge deletion evidence:** To comply with Colombian data protection regulations (Ley 1581) while respecting the Zero-Knowledge architecture, the deletion evidence table `privacy.deletion_requests` never stores cleartext `user_id`. Instead, it stores `sha256(user_id)` as 32-byte hash (`user_id_hash bytea NOT NULL CHECK (octet_length(user_id_hash) = 32)`).
* **Consents retention vs deletion:** Consents table rows are not deleted; they are marked `revoked` with timestamp and reason per legal requirement to prove compliance and consent lifecycle to authorities (Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio). All other user personal and financial records (`identity`, `bank`, `backup`) are deleted with zero remaining rows.

#### 2026-11-01 · Privacy policy and my data export (Ley 1581 de 2012)

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Antigravity. Jira: SCRUM-42, SCRUM-43. Branch: feature/privacy-policy-and-my-data. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Implement the Phase 4 compliance and privacy backend endpoints for Module M13 per `docs/modules/M13-compliance.md` and Jira SCRUM-42 (HU-028) & SCRUM-43 (HU-029):
1. `GET /privacy/policy`: public endpoint serving the active data treatment policy in Spanish (Markdown), detailing the data controller (Fynk Finance S.A.S.), authorized purposes (personal finance, informative recommendations, security/fraud prevention), data categories and storage locations following the Zero-Knowledge architecture in `CLAUDE.md`, data subject rights under Ley 1581 de 2012, contact channels, and response times (10 working days for queries, 15 working days for claims).
2. `GET /privacy/my-data`: authenticated endpoint exporting all cloud data stored for the authenticated user (Keycloak profile, registered devices, consents with audit evidence, bank links without token fingerprints, backup record count and size, and immutable audit events).

##### What was done

* **OpenAPI Contract (`contracts/modules/privacy.yaml`):**
  * Added `GET /privacy/policy` returning `PrivacyPolicy` with version `2026-11-v1`, SHA-256 integrity hash, response time guarantees, and full Spanish Markdown body.
  * Added `GET /privacy/my-data` returning `PrivacyDataExport` with schemas prefixed by `Privacy` per contract lint rules (`PrivacyProfileData`, `PrivacyDeviceData`, `PrivacyConsentData`, `PrivacyBankLinkData`, `PrivacyBackupData`, `PrivacyAuditEventData`).
  * Executed `make generate` to regenerate oapi-codegen Go server (`services/api/internal/privacy/http/api.gen.go`) and mobile TypeScript types (`apps/mobile/src/shared/api/schema.gen.ts`).
* **Identity Ports (`services/api/internal/identity/ports/ports.go`):**
  * Exported `UserProfile`, `UserProfileReader`, `NewUserProfileReader`, and `ErrUnavailable` to allow external modules (like `privacy`) to retrieve Keycloak profiles safely through module boundaries without violating `depguard`.
* **Privacy Module Implementation (`services/api/internal/privacy/`):**
  * `policy.go`: defined `CurrentPolicy()` with complete Colombian legal text under Ley 1581 de 2012 and Decreto 1377 de 2013, calculating SHA-256 checksum dynamically.
  * `collector.go`: implemented `Collector` reading user data from PostgreSQL schemas (`identity.devices`, `consents.consents`, `bank.links`, `backup.records`, `backup.recovery_keys`, and `audit.events`) while strictly suppressing any sensitive data or credential fingerprints (`token_fingerprint` omitted).
  * `server.go`: implemented `httpapi.StrictServerInterface` for `PrivacyHealth`, `GetPrivacyPolicy`, and `GetMyData`.
  * `module.go`: wired `New` and mounted routes under `requireAuth`, allowing public access to `/privacy/health` and `/privacy/policy` while protecting `/privacy/my-data`.
* **Tests:**
  * Added comprehensive unit tests in `services/api/internal/privacy/privacy_test.go` verifying policy content, legal requirements, SHA-256 integrity, data export aggregation, unauthenticated vs authenticated access, and stub route fallthrough.
  * Updated `services/api/internal/app/app_test.go` to test that `/v1/privacy/policy` is public and returns 200, while `/v1/privacy/deletion-requests` returns 501 `not_implemented`.

##### Result

* `make lint-go`: clean (0 issues).
* `make test-go test-scripts`: clean (100% passed).
* `make format-check`: all files formatted cleanly.

##### Decisions and problems

* **Depguard isolation:** Direct imports of `internal/identity/kcadmin` from `internal/privacy` were rejected by `depguard`. Created `UserProfileReader` interface and `NewUserProfileReader` constructor in `internal/identity/ports` so `privacy` accesses identity only through sanctioned port boundaries.
* **Sensitive credential suppression:** Per `M13-compliance.md` specification "bank links without fingerprints", the collector SQL query for `bank.links` explicitly selects only public metadata (`id, provider, consent_id, status, created_at, last_refreshed_at, revoked_at`) and excludes `token_fingerprint`.
* **Misspell linter on Spanish legal copy:** Marked the raw policy Markdown constant with `//nolint:misspell // Spanish copy complying with Ley 1581` to avoid false positives on legitimate Spanish legal vocabulary.

    6. Custom metric `bank.sync_failed` > 10 in 15 minutes.
    7. Bedrock API invocation errors > 5 in 15 minutes.

#### 2026-10-27 · M16 Step 1: Banco Demo data generator, OpenAPI contract and resource API

Juan Pablo Contreras (juancontrerasp) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-64. Branch: feature/bank-simulator-api-and-seed. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

Juan Pablo started module M16 (Banco Demo): "You are building module M16 of Fynk: a simulated bank, 'Banco Demo', that behaves like a real Open Finance data provider. It lets the team demo every feature with realistic data, because only Bancolombia has a sandbox in Colombia today. Fynk only reads from it. All money actions happen in the Banco Demo's own web portal, never in the Fynk app."

##### What was done

* `contracts/bank-simulator.yaml`: Defined complete OpenAPI 3.1 specification for Open Finance v1 endpoints: `GET /open-finance/v1/accounts`, `GET /open-finance/v1/accounts/{id}/transactions` (cursor pagination, `pageSize`), `DELETE /open-finance/v1/consents/{id}`, and `GET /open-finance/v1/products/cdt`, with RFC 9457 Problem Details and rate limiting headers (`429`, `Retry-After`).
* `services/bank-simulator/internal/domain/`:
  * `model.go`: Domain models for `Account`, `Transaction`, `Consent`, `CdtProduct`, `CdtDetails`, and `MinorToDecimal` COP formatting helper.
  * `generator.go`: Deterministic generator seeded by customer ID hash, covering 12 full months + current month, implementing all specified financial patterns: Salario (5.2M), Arriendo (1.4M), Servicios (186k ± 20%), 4 Subscriptions totaling 112.600 COP, Gimnasio (22.250/wk), Transporte (6.500/day), Antojos under 20k, Mercado, Restaurantes, Tarjeta payments on day 5, and CDT.
  * Opening balance adjustment at the genesis of the 12-month window ensures final balances land exactly on: Ahorros 3.200.000 COP, Billetera 4.600.000 COP, Tarjeta debt 500.000 COP, and CDT 2.000.000 COP.
  * `generator_test.go`: Tests for generator determinism, target balances, subscription totals, and formatting.
* `fixtures/demo-user.json`: Exported the canonical 12-month dataset in the shared domain shape (`Account` and `Transaction`) matching `docs/modules/00-shared-contracts.md` sections 2 and 7.
* `services/bank-simulator/internal/store/`:
  * `store.go`: Thread-safe customer data store, consent tracking, and rate limiter (60 req/min per consent).
* `services/bank-simulator/internal/api/`:
  * `handlers.go`: HTTP handlers for accounts, transactions with cursor-based pagination, consent revocation, and public CDT catalog.
  * `handlers_test.go`: Unit and contract tests covering 401 unauthorized, accounts retrieval, pagination, consent revocation returning 403 `consent_revoked`, public CDT access, and 429 rate limiting.
* `services/bank-simulator/cmd/bank-simulator/`:
  * Updated `server.go` and `main.go` to mount Open Finance routes on the HTTP listeners.

##### Result

* Tests: 11 of 11 tests pass across `cmd/bank-simulator`, `internal/api`, and `internal/domain`.
* `go vet ./...` reports 0 issues.
* `fixtures/demo-user.json` generated and verified (378 KB).
* Pull request: not opened yet (working on `feature/simulatedbank`).

##### Decisions and problems

* Go 1.27.3 was set up in `~/.local/go` to build and test the Go services.
* The generator uses SHA-256 hash of the customer ID to produce bit-for-bit identical history across runs.
* Genesis balance adjustment guarantees that summing all transactions equals the exact target balance for each account.

Key sentence: "make a log of what was instructed and what was the result so at the end we can give a project history on how was the project done and what was done in each session of each developer and each module".

##### What was done

* Read the team's own copy of the design and saved the code of the 28 screens in `docs/design/source/`, with 25 PNGs in `docs/design/screens/`.
* Rewrote `docs/design/README.md` with the tokens, the conversion from HTML, SVG and CSS animations to React Native, and the screen inventory.
* Proposed the team split: security (M1, M2), general app, dashboard and onboarding (M0, M3, M6, M7), and four groups for the other modules.
* Added the project history log: rules in CLAUDE.md, `docs/history/`, the session template, `scripts/build-history.mjs`, a pull request checklist item and a CI check in M0.

##### Result

* Developer kit with design code, images and history log, ready to copy into the repository root.

##### Decisions and problems

* One file per session instead of one shared log, so the history never causes merge conflicts.
* The 6 Fynk Plus screens in the design are not in Jira. Left as an open item.

##### Next step

M0 creates the repository with the kit in `chore/monorepo-skeleton`. Assign the modules and fill CODEOWNERS with the GitHub users.

#### 2026-10-28 · Use GitHub's stacked pull requests for dependent work

Daniel Palacios (Komi-yes) with Claude Code. Jira: SCRUM-68. Branch: docs/stacked-prs-with-gh-stack. PR: none. Status: done.

##### Instruction

While M1 was being finished, Daniel asked: "why dont you do the stacks with this https://docs.github.com/en/pull-requests/how-tos/stacked-pull-requests". After the M1 stacks merged, he asked to "tell to claude.md to do the stack of PRs with the github stack tool".

##### What was done

* **In practice:** M1's dependent pull requests became two GitHub stacks with the `gh stack` extension.
  * #81: #74 → #76 → #75 → #79 → #83 → #84.
  * #82: #77 → #78.

  Both merged bottom-up into `develop`.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Pull requests:** a rule that dependent work goes in a stack, with how a stack is targeted, reviewed and merged, and its limits.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Steps Claude Code follows:** the `gh stack` commands to start, rebase, submit and link a stack. The never-list now names `gh stack merge` next to `gh pr merge`.
* **`CLAUDE.md` › Decisions taken:** a "Stacked pull requests (28 Oct)" row.
* **`.claude/settings.json`:** `gh stack merge` is denied like `gh pr merge`, so Claude Code still can't merge.

##### Result

* `make format-check` passes.
* With the stacks, the PRs above were rebased and retargeted by GitHub after each merge. Before that, "depends on #N" pull requests on `develop` needed a manual rebase, and one failed CI because it lacked a fix from the pull request below it.

##### Decisions and problems

* **No reordering of independent work into one stack.** A stack is linear, and a pull request cannot merge before the ones under it.
* **This changes shared files owned by M0** (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/settings.json`), so Sofia reviews it.

##### Next step

Every module uses `gh stack` for its dependent pull requests.
